Genetics / every outcome of every gene

Gene census

One row for every allele presentation in the mod — every distinct outcome a registered gene can produce, with the allele pairs that produce it, what kind of gene it is, which family it sits in, which built-in breeds carry it, and the sentence the game itself shows for it. The loci that change no pixel of the coat are here too — sex, diet, appetite, the health genes — because they are the ones hardest to find any other way. Sort any column, filter to a family, tick carried by no breed for the gaps alone, or search the lot. It is meant to be read whole, by a person planning a breed or a new gene or by an AI they hand the page to: paste it in and ask which loci are still free, what already covers a rump, or which wild type a breed should sit on. The machine-readable sources behind it are the gene files themselves, common/src/main/resources/horsegenetics/genes/*.json.

What it leaves out. A wild-type row is dropped where the gene has outcomes of its own: “this gene is doing nothing” is not a presentation, and most such rows belonged to loci no breed names at all. Agouti, extension and shade keep theirs, because a horse’s base colour genuinely is one of those combinations — a chestnut is e/e. A gene that paints nothing loses only the row its author never named — the “nothing here” row, carrier pairs and all: swims like any other horse is as empty as a coat gene’s wild type, and a carrier of one copy is no less empty here than on a gene that paints. Every other outcome of such a gene stays, because the wild-type flag is also the only way to declare an outcome with no painter — it is how the diet loci, the sex locus and the body-stat genes describe outcomes that are perfectly real, and a baseline the author named rather than left blank (a mare is one) stays with them. n/n goes the way of the first rule — matched as a whole pair and never as text, so Wun/n and its relatives are untouched — except on a row that has nothing else to show, which keeps it rather than print an empty cell.

The Breeds column is what a breed’s own sheet names: its gene pools plus every strain’s, resolved through the gene rather than compared as text, so a file’s ShL/Sh finds the row that prints Sh/ShL. A breed lands on a row when any one of its pool entries produces that outcome, which is why a carrier sits on the wild-type row and a horse with two copies on the variant one. Two qualifiers narrow that claim. A name in brackets is a strain — Dhampir (White) means the locus is on that strain’s sheet and not on the breed’s own, so only founders of that strain can carry it; a breed whose own sheet names the locus is listed plain, since that sheet covers every founder. (rare) means this outcome is under 2% of that breed’s pool for that locus — a near-blank-spot worth seeing as one. The share is worked out inside the pool it came from, and for a strain it is not multiplied by how often that strain comes up: the strain name already says “only some founders”, so the two qualifiers each say their own thing and never the same thing twice. Weights in a breed file are relative, not percentages, so this is always a share of the pool rather than the number written in the file. It is not everything a horse could turn out to be: magical herds, gene splices, breeding carrots and the two editors all reach loci no breed sheet mentions, and counting those would make the column mean “possible somehow”. So a no breed cell is the blank spot this page exists to find. Two groups of loci carry a marker instead of a list, because a blank there would be a lie: the four body-stat genes are set by stat scores from the breed’s stats block rather than from any pool, and a feral only locus is forced wild on every breed and survives only on Feral Mixed.

Generated - edit nothing below the line

./gradlew :common:bakeGeneCensus writes the table, and every count in the line beneath it. It walks the registry in genotype-code order, asks each gene what every allele pair it can actually carry does, and asks each shipped breed which of those combinations its own sheet names — so a gene appears here by existing, a reworded outcome moves the day the gene file moves, and a breed joins a row the day its file names the pair. Re-run it after touching anything in horsegenetics/genes/ or horsegenetics/breeds/. A gene name is a link only where a page exists — the thirteen eye loci share Eye colour instead of having pages of their own.

On a narrow screen a pair list folds behind its own shorthand — Gld/* where one allele is in every pair, a plain count of pairs where none is, since no wildcard would be honest — and a long breed list folds behind +N more at any width. Both are visual: every pair and every breed name is in the HTML source either way, folded or not, because content a script has to reveal is content that is not there for the reader this page is written for.

1176 presentations across 333 genes. 1047 of them are carried by no built-in breed at all. 357 rows for “this locus is doing nothing” were left out - 284 wild types of genes that paint and 73 baseline rows of genes that do not - keeping the 7 that are a base colour (Agouti, Extension, Shade (ASIP/RALY)) and the 592 outcomes that paint nothing but are named all the same.

Allele pair(s) Gene Kind Category Breeds Presentation What it does
X/Y Sex Natural Other natural genes no breed Stallion One X and one Y. The horse is a stallion (a colt as a foal); its coat is not affected.
X/X Sex Natural Other natural genes no breed Mare Two X copies. The horse is a mare (a filly as a foal); its coat is not affected.
sf/sf Fertility Natural Other natural genes no breed Subfertile Two copies of sf. Every breeding is half as likely to take - even plain golden carrots sometimes come to nothing. One copy is silent.
tw/sf · tw/n Fertility Natural Other natural genes no breed Twin-prone One copy of tw. A pregnancy is twins about one time in ten instead of one in fifty.
tw/tw Fertility Natural Other natural genes no breed Strongly twin-prone Two copies of tw. A pregnancy is twins about one time in four.
Dbld/Dbld Diet Natural Other natural genes feral only Blood only Blood diet. The horse eats nothing a hand can offer and does not heal beside hay and water. Hurt, it hunts: it bites a living mob for half a heart and heals three, then leaves that animal alone for a day. It never bites another horse or anything undead, and it only goes for a player - or a tamed cat or dog - when there is nothing else within reach.
Dgem/Dgem Diet Natural Other natural genes feral only One gem only The horse will eat nothing but one gem, and refuses everything else. One is enough: it goes to full health however hurt it was. Which one it wants is fixed for the horse's life and inherited with the allele.
Ding/Ding Diet Natural Other natural genes feral only One metal only The horse will eat nothing but one metal, and refuses everything else. One is enough: it goes to full health however hurt it was. Which one it wants is fixed for the horse's life and inherited with the allele.
Dwat/Dwat Diet Natural Other natural genes feral only Water only The horse will eat nothing but water, and refuses everything else. One restores 10 hearts.
Dlava/Dlava Diet Natural Other natural genes feral only Lava only The horse will eat nothing but lava, and refuses everything else. One is enough: it goes to full health however hurt it was.
Dpot/Dpot Diet Natural Other natural genes feral only Potions only The horse will eat nothing but potions, and refuses everything else. One is enough: it goes to full health however hurt it was.
Dcake/Dcake Diet Natural Other natural genes feral only Cake only The horse will eat nothing but cake, and refuses everything else. One is enough: it goes to full health however hurt it was.
Dhum/Dhum Diet Natural Other natural genes feral only Cooked food only The horse will eat nothing but cooked food, and refuses everything else. One restores 5 hearts.
Dwht/Dwht Diet Natural Other natural genes feral only Wheat only The horse will eat nothing but wheat, and refuses everything else. One restores 10 hearts.
Dveg/Dveg Diet Natural Other natural genes feral only Raw vegetables only The horse will eat nothing but raw vegetables, and refuses everything else. One restores 10 hearts.
Dfish/Dfish Diet Natural Other natural genes feral only Fish only The horse will eat nothing but fish, and refuses everything else. One restores 10 hearts.
Dmeat/Dmeat Diet Natural Other natural genes feral only Raw meat only The horse will eat nothing but raw meat, and refuses everything else. One restores 10 hearts.
Dany/Dany Diet Natural Other natural genes feral only Anything edible The horse will eat nothing but anything edible, and refuses everything else. One restores 2 hearts.

Z/z · Z/Z Silver dapple Natural Natural dilution genes American Bashkir Curly, American Miniature, American Saddlebred, Australian Stock Horse, Chincoteague Pony, Colorado Ranger, Criollo, Falabella, Finnhorse, Jutland, Kentucky Mountain Saddle Horse, Missouri Fox Trotter, Rocky Mountain Horse, Spotted Saddle Horse, Swedish Warmblood, Trottatore Italiano, Zangersheide Silver dapple Black pigment walked toward chocolate, and most of the way to flaxen on the mane and tail, with red untouched - a chocolate body under a pale mane on a black horse, silver bay on a bay. A chestnut carrier looks unchanged.
Fl1/f Flaxen Natural Natural dilution genes American Bashkir Curly, American Saddlebred, Anadolu, Australian Stock Horse, Belgian Draft, Boerperd, Chilean Corralero, Chincoteague Pony, Colorado Ranger, Criollo, Croatian Coldblood, Danubian, Finnhorse, Florida Cracker Horse, Gelderlander, Gotland Pony, Haflinger, Highland Pony, Jutland, Kentucky Mountain Saddle Horse, Mangalarga Marchador, Missouri Fox Trotter, Mongolian Horse, Morgan, Newfoundland Pony, Novooleksandrivsky Heavy Draught, Oldenburg, Pindos, Pony of the Americas, Posavac, Rocky Mountain Horse, Sanhe, Selle Français, Spotted Saddle Horse, Swedish Warmblood, Trottatore Italiano, Ukrainian Riding Horse, Vyatka, Walkaloosa, Yili, Yugoslavian Trotter, Zangersheide Mild flaxen Red long hair shot through with gold and cream strands - enough to notice, not enough to call the horse flaxen at a distance.
Fl1/Fl1 · Fl2/f Flaxen Natural Natural dilution genes American Bashkir Curly, American Saddlebred, Australian Stock Horse, Belgian Draft, Boerperd, Chincoteague Pony, Criollo, Croatian Coldblood, Finnhorse, Florida Cracker Horse (rare), Gelderlander, Gotland Pony, Haflinger, Highland Pony, Jutland, Kentucky Mountain Saddle Horse, Morgan, Newfoundland Pony, Novooleksandrivsky Heavy Draught, Posavac, Rocky Mountain Horse Flaxen Clearly blond mane, tail and forelock over a red body: the classic flaxen chestnut.
Fl2/Fl1 Flaxen Natural Natural dilution genes Belgian Draft, Finnhorse, Haflinger, Jutland, Kentucky Mountain Saddle Horse, Morgan Strong flaxen Pale cream long hair against the body colour, with the hard contrast a Haflinger is known for.
Fl2/Fl2 Flaxen Natural Natural dilution genes Finnhorse, Haflinger, Jutland Extreme flaxen A near-white fall of mane and tail, with only a few darker strands left in it.
Mu/Mu Mushroom Natural Natural dilution genes no breed Mushroom Red pigment cut hard and partly traded for black, so a chestnut becomes a flat sepia-khaki. A black or bay horse has little red to lose and looks much the same.
d1/d2 · d1/d1 Dun Natural Natural dilution genes Akhal-Teke, American Bashkir Curly, American Miniature, Anadolu, Australian Stock Horse, Banker Horse, Boerperd, Chilean Corralero, Chincoteague Pony, Colorado Ranger, Criollo (Traditional Dun Criollo, Broad-Colour Criollo), Croatian Coldblood, Domestic Mountain Pony, Exmoor Pony, Florida Cracker Horse, Gotland Pony, Highland Pony, Hucul Pony, Kentucky Mountain Saddle Horse, Kiger Mustang, Kiso Horse, Mongolian Horse, Mustang, Newfoundland Pony, Nokota, Norwegian Fjord, Pony of the Americas, Quarter Horse, Rocky Mountain Horse, Tarpan, Vyatka, Yakutian Horse, Yonaguni Horse Primitive markings No real dilution - the horse is its base colour - but a dorsal stripe still runs from poll to tail, a shade darker than the body around it, and faint leg bars and a shoulder shadow may come with it on any base pale enough to show them. One d1 copy is enough; it is how a plain bay or chestnut ends up with primitive markings and no dun.
D/d2 · D/d1 · D/D Dun Natural Natural dilution genes American Bashkir Curly, American Miniature, American Paint, American Saddlebred, Anadolu, Australian Stock Horse, Banker Horse, Boerperd, Chilean Corralero, Chincoteague Pony, Colorado Ranger, Connemara Pony, Criollo (Traditional Dun Criollo, Broad-Colour Criollo), Croatian Coldblood, Dales Pony, Domestic Mountain Pony, Falabella, Finnhorse (rare), Florida Cracker Horse, Gotland Pony, Gypsy Vanner, Henson Horse, Highland Pony, Hucul Pony, Icelandic Horse, Irish Draught, Kentucky Mountain Saddle Horse, Kiger Mustang, Kiso Horse, Konik, Mangalarga Marchador, Marwari, Missouri Fox Trotter, Mongolian Horse, Morgan, Mustang, New Forest Pony, Newfoundland Pony, Nokota, Norwegian Fjord, Paso Fino, Pony of the Americas, Przewalski's Horse, Puerto Rican Paso Fino, Riwoche Horse, Rocky Mountain Horse, Selle Français, Shetland Pony, Sorraia, Spotted Saddle Horse, Standardbred, Tarpan, Trottatore Italiano, Vyatka, Walkaloosa, Welsh Pony, Yakutian Horse, Zangersheide Dun Black pigment thins while red is left alone, so a bay turns tan, a black turns blue-grey grullo, and a chestnut - which has no black to lose - stays very nearly chestnut. The points, a dorsal stripe from poll to tail, and whichever primitive markings this horse drew skip the dilution and stay dark.
Brn/Brn Brindle (MBTPS2) Natural Natural coat genes no breed Brindle Irregular white streaks, soft-edged and broken, running down from the topline over the barrel, quarters and neck and turning crosswise on the upper legs. They are white on any base colour, and they do not match from one side of the horse to the other. It takes two copies, so every brindle horse is a mare.
S1/s Sooty Natural Natural coat genes no breed Faint sooty A dark haze along the spine and a little shading over the croup. Easy to miss, and easy to mistake for a dun's dorsal stripe - which it is not: there are no leg bars, no shoulder bar and no mask with it.
S1/S1 · S2/s Sooty Natural Natural coat genes no breed Sooty An obviously darker topline, crest and upper barrel over a clearer belly and flank - the countershading the word usually means.
S2/S1 Sooty Natural Natural coat genes no breed Heavy sooty A dark cape over the shoulder, back and croup, with the base colour left showing mostly low on the sides. On a chestnut-based coat the lower legs go muddy with it.
S2/S2 Sooty Natural Natural coat genes no breed Extreme sooty Dark over nearly the whole body. A bay reads as brown or black, a buckskin as a dark bay, and a palomino as a bronze horse - which is the commonest reason a coat cannot be identified from a photograph.
Pa1/pa Pangare (mealy) Natural Natural coat genes Debao Pony, Exmoor Pony, Icelandic Horse, Norwegian Fjord, Shetland Pony, Suffolk Punch Trace pangare A pale lower muzzle and faint rings round the eyes, and very little else. Easy to mistake for ordinary shade variation, which is why a pale muzzle on its own does not identify the trait.
Pa1/Pa1 · Pa2/pa Pangare (mealy) Natural Natural coat genes Exmoor Pony, Icelandic Horse, Norwegian Fjord, Shetland Pony, Suffolk Punch Mild pangare A pale muzzle and clear eye rings, a light underline, and soft pale patches behind the elbows.
Pa2/Pa1 Pangare (mealy) Natural Natural coat genes Exmoor Pony, Icelandic Horse, Norwegian Fjord Pangare The familiar mealy horse: pale muzzle and jaw, an obviously light belly, groin and flank, and visible lightening up the inside of the legs.
Pa2/Pa2 Pangare (mealy) Natural Natural coat genes Exmoor Pony, Norwegian Fjord Strong pangare Broad cream soft areas - the pale lower face reaching up the cheeks, a creamy underside climbing the barrel, and the insides of the legs pale most of the way up. The outer legs and any black points stay dark.
sno/sno · sun/sno · prl/sno · Cr/sno · Cr/sun · Cr/prl · Cr/Cr MATP (cream / pearl) Natural Natural dilution genes Akhal-Teke, American Miniature (rare), American Paint (rare), American Saddlebred (rare), Andalusian (rare), Appaloosa (rare), Banker Horse (rare), Boerperd (rare), Canadian Horse (rare), Chilean Corralero (rare), Chincoteague Pony, Colorado Ranger (rare), Connemara Pony (rare), Criollo (Traditional Dun Criollo, Broad-Colour Criollo), Dolahest (rare), Falabella (rare), Gypsy Vanner (rare), Highland Pony (rare), Icelandic Horse (rare), Irish Draught (rare), Karabakh (rare), Kentucky Mountain Saddle Horse, Kiger Mustang (rare), Lusitano (rare), Mangalarga Marchador, Marwari (rare), Missouri Fox Trotter, Mongolian Horse (rare), Morgan (rare), Mustang (rare), New Forest Pony (rare), Norwegian Fjord (rare), Oldenburg (rare), Paso Fino (rare), Pony of the Americas (rare), Puerto Rican Paso Fino (rare), Quarter Horse (rare), Rocky Mountain Horse (rare), Shetland Pony (rare), Spotted Saddle Horse (rare), Standardbred (rare), Tennessee Walking Horse (rare), Vyatka (rare), Walkaloosa (rare), Welsh Pony (rare) Double dilute Red almost entirely gone and black heavily cut - cremello, perlino or smoky cream, a near-white body over rusty points. Two cream copies do it, and so does one cream with one pearl.
sun/sun MATP (cream / pearl) Natural Natural dilution genes no breed Sunshine Two sunshine copies. A whole-coat dilution that reads closer to champagne than to a cremello - warm and light rather than washed out - and, unlike the double dilutes, it does not give the horse a pale blue eye.
prl/sun · prl/prl MATP (cream / pearl) Natural Natural dilution genes Andalusian (rare), Gypsy Vanner (rare), Lusitano (rare) Classic pearl Black cut hard and red barely touched - a warm, lightened coat that keeps its colour rather than washing out. Only two pearl copies and no cream produce it.
Cr/N MATP (cream / pearl) Natural Natural dilution genes Akhal-Teke, American Bashkir Curly, American Miniature, American Paint, American Saddlebred, Andalusian, Appaloosa, Australian Stock Horse, Banker Horse, Boerperd, Canadian Horse (rare), Chilean Corralero, Chincoteague Pony, Colorado Ranger, Connemara Pony, Criollo (Traditional Dun Criollo, Broad-Colour Criollo), Debao Pony, Dolahest, Falabella, Finnhorse, Gotland Pony, Gypsy Vanner, Highland Pony, Icelandic Horse, Irish Draught (rare), Karabakh, Kentucky Mountain Saddle Horse, Kiger Mustang, Lusitano, Mangalarga Marchador, Marwari, Missouri Fox Trotter, Mongolian Horse, Morgan, Mustang, New Forest Pony (rare), Nokota, Norwegian Fjord, Oldenburg, Paso Fino, Pony of the Americas, Puerto Rican Paso Fino, Quarter Horse, Rocky Mountain Horse, Selle Français, Shetland Pony, Spotted Saddle Horse, Standardbred, Swedish Warmblood, Tennessee Walking Horse, Thoroughbred (rare), Trottatore Italiano, Vyatka, Walkaloosa, Welsh Pony, Wielkopolski, Zangersheide Single cream Red cut hard and black cut a little - palomino on a chestnut, buckskin on a bay, smoky black on a black. The points go dark brown, never jet black.
Ch/c · Ch/Ch Champagne Natural Natural dilution genes American Bashkir Curly, American Cream Draft, American Miniature, American Saddlebred, Australian Stock Horse, Colorado Ranger, Falabella, Kentucky Mountain Saddle Horse, Missouri Fox Trotter, Mustang, Rocky Mountain Horse, Spotted Saddle Horse, Tennessee Walking Horse, Trottatore Italiano Champagne Red mostly kept and black cut hard, with some of the removed black fed back as red - gold champagne on a chestnut, classic taupe on a black, amber with chocolate points on a bay. One copy and two look the same. The eye is diluted too, landing anywhere from amber through hazel to a rare olive green, and which one is inherited with the allele.
G2/N Grey Natural Natural dilution genes American Miniature, American Saddlebred, Anadolu, Andalusian, Baise Horse, Barb, Boerperd, Chilean Corralero, Colorado Ranger, Connemara Pony, Criollo, Croatian Coldblood, Debao Pony, Dolahest, Florida Cracker Horse, Gelderlander, Guoxia, Hequ, Highland Pony, Jutland, Karabair, Kentucky Mountain Saddle Horse, Mangalarga Marchador, Medimurje Horse, Missouri Fox Trotter, Mongolian Horse, Montenegrin Domestic Hilly Horse, Mustang, Newfoundland Pony, Nokota, Oldenburg, Orlov Trotter, Pindos, Pony of the Americas, Posavac, Quarter Horse, Rocky Mountain Horse, Selle Français, Spotted Saddle Horse, Swedish Warmblood, Tennessee Walking Horse, Tersk, Trottatore Italiano, Welsh Pony, Wielkopolski, Yakutian Horse, Yili, Yugoslavian Trotter, Zangersheide Slow grey (N/G2) One duplication, and the slowest greying there is: this horse spends its life as a dark steel or mid dapple grey with its base colour still legible under the white hairs. A foal is born its base colour and greys on reaching adulthood; the head goes first, the legs and the long hair last.

A foal is born its base colour and greys on reaching adulthood; the head goes first, the legs and the long hair last.
G3/G2 Grey Natural Natural dilution genes Andalusian, Barb, Connemara Pony, Kladruber (Grey), Mangalarga Marchador, Montenegrin Domestic Hilly Horse, Orlov Trotter, Tersk, Welsh Pony, Yakutian Horse Fast grey (G2/G3) Three copies between the two alleles. A light grey: dapples fading out, base colour left mostly on the legs and around the flank. A foal is born its base colour and greys on reaching adulthood; the head goes first, the legs and the long hair last.
G3/G3 Grey Natural Natural dilution genes Andalusian, Anglo-Arabian, Arabian, Azteca, Barb, Camargue, Connemara Pony, Kaimanawa, Karabair, Kentucky Mountain Saddle Horse, Kladruber (Grey), Lipizzan, Lusitano, Mangalarga Marchador, Mongolian Horse, Montenegrin Domestic Hilly Horse, Orlov Trotter, Percheron, Tersk, Welsh Pony, Yakutian Horse Rapid grey (G3/G3) Two triplications, the fastest greying and the highest melanoma risk. Very nearly white - the dark skin and dark eyes are what still say grey rather than white. A foal is born its base colour and greys on reaching adulthood; the head goes first, the legs and the long hair last.
TE2/TE2 Tiger eye (SLC24A5) Natural Other natural genes no breed Tiger eye (yellow) The paler, more yellow shade two TE2 copies produce - the same trait a stop lighter than the amber TE1 gives.
TE1/TE2 · TE1/TE1 Tiger eye (SLC24A5) Natural Other natural genes Puerto Rican Paso Fino Tiger eye (amber) A bright amber iris, the colour of a hawk's eye, in a horse whose coat is entirely ordinary. Nothing else about the horse changes.
Inv/Inv Right eye colour Natural Other natural genes no breed Invisible eye No iris is painted at all - the coat on the head shows straight through where the eye should be. Nothing further down this eye's stack shows either: no sector, no glow.
Cha/Cha Right eye colour Natural Other natural genes no breed Chaos eye A colour written on the allele copy rather than on the allele, so no two chaos-eyed horses match and a line of them drifts.
Red/Red Right eye colour Natural Other natural genes Dhampir Red eye A deep blood red - the dhampir's eye. With a glowing sclera beside it, it is the face the Dhampir breed is known by; on its own it is simply a rare and unsettling horse.

M1/m Markings Natural Other natural genes no breed Minimal markings A star, or nothing more than a coronet above a hoof. The commonest thing a horse can have and still be called solid.

C/C DMRT3 (gait) Natural Natural health genes American Saddlebred, Arabian, Boerperd, Icelandic Horse (rare), Kentucky Mountain Saddle Horse (rare), Mangalarga Marchador (rare), Missouri Fox Trotter (rare), Morgan, Przewalski's Horse, Quarter Horse, Rocky Mountain Horse (rare), Spotted Saddle Horse (rare), Tennessee Walking Horse (rare), Thoroughbred, Trottatore Italiano (rare), Walkaloosa (rare), Yugoslavian Trotter No gait copy No gait copy: walk, trot and canter, the ordinary horse.

Zeb/n Zebra striping Natural Natural white genes no breed Shadow-striped One copy suppresses pigment part of the way, so the horse wears the full zebra map as faint bands a shade off its own colour rather than as white ones - a plains zebra's shadow stripes. Visible, and worth breeding from.
Zeb/Zeb Zebra striping Natural Natural white genes no breed Zebra-striped White stripes taken out of the horse's own colour: vertical bands off the spine, arcs round the hip, rings down the legs, narrow bands on the neck and face, a dark dorsal stripe and muzzle, and a pale belly. Black and white on a black horse, red and white on a chestnut, gold and white on a palomino.
Rn/rn · Rn/Rn Roan Natural Natural white genes American Bashkir Curly, American Miniature, American Saddlebred, Appaloosa, Australian Stock Horse, Belgian Draft, Boerperd, Canadian Horse, Chilean Corralero, Chincoteague Pony, Colorado Ranger, Criollo, Croatian Coldblood, Dales Pony, Dartmoor Pony, Finnhorse, Florida Cracker Horse, Guoxia, Hackney, Haflinger, Hanoverian, Icelandic Horse, Irish Draught, Jutland, Kentucky Mountain Saddle Horse, Mangalarga Marchador, Missouri Fox Trotter, Mongolian Horse, Morgan, Murgese, Mustang, New Forest Pony, Newfoundland Pony, Nokota, Novooleksandrivsky Heavy Draught, Oldenburg, Paso Fino, Pindos, Pony of the Americas, Quarter Horse, Rocky Mountain Horse, Selle Français, Spotted Saddle Horse, Standardbred, Tennessee Walking Horse, Thoroughbred, Trakehner, Trottatore Italiano, Walkaloosa, Welsh Pony, Yakutian Horse, Zangersheide Roan An even salt-and-pepper of white hairs through the whole trunk - barrel, shoulder, back, flank and hip alike - over a base colour it does not change: blue roan on a black, strawberry on a chestnut. The head stays dark, giving the mask classic roan is known by, the mane and tail stay solid, and the dark lower leg rises into the roaning in a point.
Rb/rb Rabicano Natural Natural white genes American Bashkir Curly, American Saddlebred, Anadolu, Anglo-Arabian, Australian Stock Horse, Azteca, Boerperd, Chilean Corralero, Chincoteague Pony, Colorado Ranger, Criollo, Croatian Coldblood, Danubian, Finnhorse, Florida Cracker Horse, Gelderlander, Mangalarga Marchador, Missouri Fox Trotter, Mongolian Horse, Nokota, Oldenburg, Sanhe, Selle Français, Spotted Saddle Horse, Swedish Warmblood, Trottatore Italiano, Ukrainian Riding Horse, Vyatka, Yili, Yugoslavian Trotter, Zangersheide Rabicano White ticking from the tail dock and flank forward - a frosted, banded coon tail and a scatter of white hairs over the rear barrel and belly. How much shows varies enormously and can be almost nothing: a horse recorded as solid can carry it and throw a strongly ticked foal.
Rb/Rb Rabicano Natural Natural white genes no breed Strong rabicano Two copies. The same pattern with its floor raised - a horse that never comes out entirely plain, often with broken vertical rib bars up the barrel and enough white to be mistaken for a roan until you look at its tail.
To/to · To/To Tobiano Natural Natural white genes American Bashkir Curly, American Miniature, American Paint, American Saddlebred, Australian Stock Horse, Azteca (American Patterned), Chincoteague Pony (Traditional Assateague, Sport Type), Clydesdale, Debao Pony, Falabella, Finnhorse (rare), Florida Cracker Horse, Gelderlander, Gypsy Vanner, Hucul Pony, Icelandic Horse, Mangalarga Marchador, Marwari, Missouri Fox Trotter, Mongolian Horse, Mustang, Oldenburg, Selle Français, Shetland Pony, Spotted Saddle Horse, Swedish Warmblood, Trottatore Italiano, Yakutian Horse, Zangersheide Tobiano Large, smooth-edged white patches that run up and over the topline, flowing unbroken from the barrel down the legs. The legs tend to white; the head stays coloured.
LP/lp · LP/LP Leopard complex (appaloosa) Natural Natural white genes American Bashkir Curly, Appaloosa, Colorado Ranger (Leopard Rangerbred), Kiger Mustang, Knabstrupper, Mustang, Pony of the Americas, Shetland Pony, Tarpan, Walkaloosa, Yakutian Horse Varnish roan Two leopard-complex copies and no modifier: heavy, uneven whitening that leaves darker “varnish marks” over the horse's bones - cheekbones, shoulder blades, elbows, hips, stifles and knees - so it ends up outlined by its own anatomy. Not true roan and not a stronger version of it: patchy where classic roan is even, and it whitens the head, which classic roan never does.
O/N EDNRB (frame overo) Natural Natural white genes American Bashkir Curly, American Paint, American Saddlebred, Australian Stock Horse, Azteca (American Patterned), Chincoteague Pony, Criollo, Missouri Fox Trotter, Nokota, Selle Français (rare), Spotted Saddle Horse, Trottatore Italiano (rare), Zangersheide (rare) Frame overo Big, sharp-edged splotches of white across the middle of the side - barrel, flank, shoulder and lower neck - that never cross the back and rarely reach the belly, so the coloured coat is left framing them above and below, which is where the name comes from. The face is broadly white, often bald, and the legs stay dark but for the occasional coronet. The two sides need not match. Some carriers are marked so little you would never guess, which is why frame is tested for and not eyeballed.
O/O EDNRB (frame overo) Natural Natural white genes no breed Lethal white An all-white foal with pink skin - Overo Lethal White Syndrome, which two frame carriers produce one time in four. In a real horse the gut has no nerve supply and the foal dies within a day or two; this mod paints the coat and does not yet model the death.
W34/N · W32/N · W35/N · W20/N KIT (white spotting) Natural Natural white genes American Bashkir Curly, American Miniature, American Paint, American Saddlebred, Appaloosa, Arabian, Australian Stock Horse, Azteca (American Patterned), Banker Horse, Caspian Pony, Chincoteague Pony (Traditional Assateague), Clydesdale, Falabella, Gelderlander, Gypsy Vanner, Morgan, Mustang, Paso Fino, Quarter Horse, Shetland Pony, Shire, Spotted Saddle Horse, Standardbred, Tennessee Walking Horse, Thoroughbred, Trottatore Italiano, Welsh Pony, Zangersheide Minimal white Ordinary-looking white: a star or snip and a low sock or two, the sort of marking nobody would call a pattern. This is what a single booster copy - W20, W35, W32 or W34 - usually does on its own. They are boosters, and they show properly beside another variant.
W34/W34 · W32/W34 · W32/W32 · W35/W34 · W35/W32 · W35/W35 · W20/W34 · W20/W32 · W20/W35 · W20/W20 KIT (white spotting) Natural Natural white genes no breed Modest white A little more than ordinary: a narrow blaze, socks climbing past the fetlock, maybe a fleck under the belly. Two booster copies are viable - the same one twice or two different ones - and do add white, but not reliably twice as much as one.
SB1/N KIT (white spotting) Natural Natural white genes American Bashkir Curly, American Miniature, American Paint, American Saddlebred, Anadolu, Anglo-Arabian, Appaloosa, Arabian, Australian Stock Horse, Azteca (Mexican Solid, American Patterned), Banker Horse, Caspian Pony, Chilean Corralero, Chincoteague Pony (Traditional Assateague, Sport Type), Clydesdale, Colorado Ranger, Criollo, Croatian Coldblood, Danubian, Debao Pony, Dolahest, Falabella, Fell Pony (rare), Florida Cracker Horse, Gelderlander, Gypsy Vanner, Mangalarga Marchador, Missouri Fox Trotter, Mongolian Horse, Morgan, Mustang, Nokota, Oldenburg, Posavac, Quarter Horse, Sanhe, Selle Français, Shetland Pony, Shire, Spotted Saddle Horse, Standardbred, Swedish Warmblood, Tennessee Walking Horse, Trottatore Italiano, Ukrainian Riding Horse, Vyatka, Welsh Pony, Yakutian Horse, Yili, Yugoslavian Trotter, Zangersheide Sabino Classic sabino: tall jagged stockings, white up the belly, a broad blaze, and roaned ragged margins rather than the clean edge a splash marking leaves.

Clydesdale, Shire Near-white Ninety per cent white or more, with a few coloured flecks left on the ears and the crest. Sabino-white, and where the strong W alleles land when they meet each other.
W19/N · W19/W34 · W19/W32 · W19/W35 · W19/W20 · W15/W34 · W15/W32 · W15/W35 · W15/W20 · W38/W34 · W38/W32 · W38/W35 · W38/W20 · W37/N · W37/W34 · W37/W32 · W37/W35 · W37/W20 · W33/W34 · W33/W32 · W33/W35 · W33/W20 · W31/W34 · W31/W32 · W31/W35 · W31/W20 · W28/W34 · W28/W32 · W28/W35 · W28/W20 · W26/N · W26/W34 · W26/W32 · W26/W35 · W26/W20 · W12/W34 · W12/W32 · W12/W35 · W12/W20 · W6/W34 · W6/W32 · W6/W35 · W6/W20 · W5/W34 · W5/W32 · W5/W35 · W5/W20 · W10/W34 · W10/W32 · W10/W35 · W10/W20 · W39/N · W30/N · W27/N · W25/N · W16/N · W7/N · W3/N · W1/N · W23/N · W13/N KIT (white spotting) Natural Natural white genes American Miniature, American Paint (rare), Arabian (rare), Shetland Pony, Thoroughbred (rare) Extensive white Most of the horse is white, with colour holding out over the topline, the crest and the quarters. The margins are still ragged - this is a very white spotted horse, not a white one.
W21/N · W21/W34 · W21/W32 · W21/W35 · W21/W20 · W21/SB1 · W18/N · W18/W34 · W18/W32 · W18/W35 · W18/W20 · W18/SB1 · W8/N · W8/W34 · W8/W32 · W8/W35 · W8/W20 · W8/SB1 KIT (white spotting) Natural Natural white genes Icelandic Horse (rare) Speckled white White flecks and mottling scattered through the coat, thickest low on the body and thinning toward the topline, with a white face - not the torn-edged patches of sabino. The Icelandic word for it is yruskjottur.
W24/N · W24/W34 · W24/W32 · W24/W35 · W24/W20 · W24/SB1 · W24/W19 · W24/W15 · W24/W38 · W24/W37 · W24/W33 · W24/W31 · W24/W28 · W24/W26 · W24/W21 · W24/W18 · W24/W12 · W24/W8 · W24/W6 · W24/W5 · · W11/SB1 · W11/W19 · W11/W15 · W11/W38 · W11/W37 · W11/W33 · W11/W31 · W11/W28 · W11/W26 · W11/W21 · W11/W18 · W11/W12 · W11/W8 · W11/W6 · W11/W5 · W11/W10 · W11/W39 · W11/W30 · W11/W27 · W11/W25 · · W9/W26 · W9/W21 · W9/W18 · W9/W12 · W9/W8 · W9/W6 · W9/W5 · W9/W10 · W9/W39 · W9/W30 · W9/W27 · W9/W25 · W9/W16 · W9/W7 · W9/W3 · W9/W1 · W9/W23 · W9/W13 · W9/W24 · W9/W17 · W9/W14 · W9/W11 · W2/N · W2/W34 · W2/W32 · W2/W35 · W2/W20 · W2/SB1 · W2/W19 · W2/W15 · W2/W38 · W2/W37 · W2/W33 · W2/W31 · W2/W28 · W2/W26 · W2/W21 · W2/W18 · W2/W12 · W2/W8 · W2/W6 · W2/W5 · W2/W10 · W2/W39 · W2/W30 · W2/W27 · W2/W25 · W2/W16 · W2/W7 · W2/W3 · W2/W1 · W2/W23 · W2/W13 · W2/W24 · W2/W17 · W2/W14 · W2/W11 · W2/W9 · W4/W24 · W4/W17 · W4/W14 · W4/W11 · W4/W9 · W4/W2 · W22/W34 · W22/W32 · W22/W35 · W22/W20 · W22/W19 · W22/W37 · W22/W26 · W22/W24 · W22/W17 · W22/W14 · W22/W11 · W22/W9 · W22/W2 KIT (white spotting) Natural Natural white genes Thoroughbred (rare) Dominant white Every pigment gone over the whole body and pink skin underneath, so the horse renders pure white and no other coat gene it carries can be seen. This is an absence of pigment cells, not a great deal of sabino.
W4/N · W4/W34 · W4/W32 · W4/W35 · W4/W20 · W4/SB1 · W4/W19 · W4/W15 · W4/W38 · W4/W37 · W4/W33 · W4/W31 · W4/W28 · W4/W26 · W4/W21 · W4/W18 · W4/W12 · W4/W8 · W4/W6 · W4/W5 · W4/W10 · W4/W39 · W4/W30 · W4/W27 · W4/W25 · W4/W16 · W4/W7 · W4/W3 · W4/W1 · W4/W23 · W4/W13 · W4/W4 · W22/W4 KIT (white spotting) Natural Natural white genes Morgan Camarillo white White from birth over the whole body, with pink skin underneath - not a grey that turned white, and not a great deal of sabino. Every other coat gene it carries is hidden and still inherited. Unlike the other all-white variant at this locus, two copies are not known to be nonviable.
ma/ma Manchado Natural Natural white genes no breed Manchado Broad, clean white over the back, crest, shoulder and croup, with smooth rounded islands of the base colour left inside it and a mostly white tail. The head, the underside of the neck, the belly and the lower legs stay coloured - the pattern is top-down, which is what separates it from splash.
SW9/N · SW5/N MITF (splash white) Natural Natural white genes Andalusian (rare) Splash white As if the horse had been dipped in white paint to just above the knee: high leg white with a clean, sharply bounded edge, white up the belly and a broad blaze. How much varies enormously between horses with the same genotype.
SW9/SW9 · SW5/SW9 · SW5/SW5 · SW1/SW9 · SW1/SW5 · SW1/SW1 · SW3/N MITF (splash white) Natural Natural white genes no breed Bold splash white The same pattern carried up the barrel: white well past the elbow and stifle, a bald face, and only the topline and quarters left coloured. Two copies of SW1 land here, and so does a single SW3.
SW1/N MITF (splash white) Natural Natural white genes American Bashkir Curly, American Miniature, American Paint, American Saddlebred, Appaloosa, Australian Stock Horse, Azteca (American Patterned), Chincoteague Pony (Traditional Assateague, Sport Type), Cleveland Bay, Clydesdale, Criollo, Falabella, Finnhorse, Florida Cracker Horse, Gelderlander, Gypsy Vanner, Hackney, Hanoverian, Icelandic Horse, Knabstrupper, Missouri Fox Trotter, Mongolian Horse, Morgan, Nokota, Oldenburg, Quarter Horse, Selle Français, Shetland Pony, Shire, Spotted Saddle Horse, Swedish Warmblood, Tennessee Walking Horse, Thoroughbred, Trakehner, Trottatore Italiano, Welsh Pony, Zangersheide Minimal splash white Usually no more than a star or a snip and a clean-edged sock or two - markings you would not look at twice - and sometimes a blue eye beside them. But the same single copy occasionally draws a broad blaze, high white and a belly, so two horses with this genotype need not look remotely alike.
SW3/SW9 · SW3/SW5 · SW3/SW1 MITF (splash white) Natural Natural white genes no breed Extensive splash white Almost the whole horse below the topline is white, with colour surviving only along the spine and over the ears. This is where two different MITF variants meet.
SW10/N · SW4/N · SW2/N PAX3 (splash white) Natural Natural white genes American Paint, Andalusian (rare), Appaloosa, Lipizzan, Quarter Horse, Spotted Saddle Horse Splash white Splash-type white: leg white with a clean edge, white up the belly and a bold face marking. On its own it is hard to tell from the MITF kind - the two loci make the same pattern, which is why they were long taken for one gene.
SW4/SW10 · SW2/SW10 · SW2/SW4 · SW2/SW2 PAX3 (splash white) Natural Natural white genes no breed Bold splash white White carried well up the barrel and over the face. Two copies of SW2 are viable and land here - and, like any doubled splash, deaf: the pigment cells that never reached the coat never reached the inner ear either.
T/T MSTN (myostatin) Natural Natural health genes no breed Stayer Two stayer copies - the baseline horse. No speed bonus, and no hearts given up for one.
C/T MSTN (myostatin) Natural Natural health genes no breed Middle distance One of each. Half a sprinter's speed for half a sprinter's hearts - the alleles do not hide each other, they add.
C/C MSTN (myostatin) Natural Natural health genes no breed Sprinter Two sprint copies. The fastest horse this locus can make, and the one with the fewest hearts to show for it.
G/G PDK4 Natural Natural health genes no breed PDK4 wild type No racing copy. No contribution to speed.
A/G PDK4 Natural Natural health genes no breed PDK4 intermediate One racing copy. Exactly half the bonus - the alleles add rather than one masking the other.
A/A PDK4 Natural Natural health genes no breed PDK4 racing type Two racing copies. The full speed bonus this locus offers.
C/C CKM Natural Natural health genes no breed CKM wild type No racing copy. No contribution to speed.
T/C CKM Natural Natural health genes no breed CKM intermediate One racing copy, half the contribution.
T/T CKM Natural Natural health genes no breed CKM racing type Two racing copies. The full contribution this locus makes to speed.
n/n RYR2 (jumping) Natural Natural health genes no breed Wild type No jumping copy. Baseline jump strength.
J/n RYR2 (jumping) Natural Natural health genes no breed Part jumper One jumping copy, half the bonus.
J/J RYR2 (jumping) Natural Natural health genes no breed Jumper Two jumping copies. The full bonus to jump strength.
n/n LCORL / NCAPG (height) Natural Natural health genes no breed Wild type No tall copy. Ordinary height.
L/n LCORL / NCAPG (height) Natural Natural health genes no breed Part tall One tall copy. Half the height, half the stride - the alleles add.
L/L LCORL / NCAPG (height) Natural Natural health genes no breed Tall Two tall copies. A visibly larger horse, a longer stride and a higher jump.
N/N HMGA2 (pony) Natural Natural health genes no breed Wild type No pony copy. Ordinary height.
p/N HMGA2 (pony) Natural Natural health genes no breed Part pony One pony copy. Half the loss of height and half the gain in hearts.
p/p HMGA2 (pony) Natural Natural health genes no breed Pony Two pony copies. A visibly smaller, slower, lower-jumping horse - and the hardiest one this locus makes.
D5/N · D4/N · D3/N · D2/N · D1/N ACAN Natural Natural health genes American Miniature, Chincoteague Pony, Friesian ACAN carrier One broken copy and one working one. The horse is a normal size and there is no way to see it. Two carriers bred together throw a dwarf one time in four - and it does not matter whether they carry the same variant.
D5/D5 · D4/D5 · D4/D4 · D3/D5 · D3/D4 · D3/D3 · D2/D5 · D2/D4 · D2/D3 · D2/D2 · D1/D5 · D1/D4 · D1/D3 · D1/D2 ACAN Natural Natural health genes no breed Chondrodysplastic dwarfism No working copy of ACAN. The horse is markedly small with shortened limbs, moves badly, jumps badly, and has far fewer hearts than it should.
D1/D1 ACAN Natural Natural health genes no breed Lethal dwarfism (D1/D1) Two copies of the severe D1 variant. The foal is born profoundly malformed and does not survive.
d/N B4GALT7 Natural Natural health genes no breed B4GALT7 carrier One copy of d. The horse is completely normal and there is no way to see it - but half its foals inherit the copy, and two carriers bred together are the only way friesian dwarfism appears.
d/d B4GALT7 Natural Natural health genes no breed Friesian dwarfism Short, splayed limbs and a weak ribcage on a normal-sized head. The horse lives, but it is small, slow, jumps badly and has noticeably fewer hearts.
ffs/N PLOD1 Natural Natural health genes Gelderlander, Oldenburg, Selle Français, Swedish Warmblood PLOD1 carrier One copy of ffs. The horse is completely normal and there is no way to see it - but half its foals inherit the copy, and two carriers bred together are the only way fragile foal syndrome appears.
ffs/ffs PLOD1 Natural Natural health genes no breed Fragile foal syndrome A collagen defect: the skin tears at a touch and the joints will not hold. The foal is born alive and does not survive it.
efih/N RAPGEF5 Natural Natural health genes no breed RAPGEF5 carrier One copy of efih. The horse is completely normal and there is no way to see it - but half its foals inherit the copy, and two carriers bred together are the only way hypoparathyroidism (efih) appears.
efih/efih RAPGEF5 Natural Natural health genes no breed Hypoparathyroidism (EFIH) The foal is born with no parathyroid glands and cannot hold its calcium. It seizes and does not survive.
nfs/N ST14 Natural Natural health genes no breed ST14 carrier One copy of nfs. The horse is completely normal and there is no way to see it - but half its foals inherit the copy, and two carriers bred together are the only way naked foal syndrome appears.
nfs/nfs ST14 Natural Natural health genes no breed Naked foal syndrome The foal is born with almost no hair and cannot hold its own temperature. It does not survive. (The bare coat itself is not drawn yet.)
sa/N SHOX (skeletal atavism) Natural Natural health genes no breed SHOX (skeletal atavism) carrier One copy of sa. The horse is completely normal and there is no way to see it - but half its foals inherit the copy, and two carriers bred together are the only way skeletal atavism appears.
sa/sa SHOX (skeletal atavism) Natural Natural health genes no breed Skeletal atavism The splint bones grow out to their full ancestral length and the limbs come out malformed. The foal cannot stand and does not survive.
met/N MET Natural Natural health genes no breed MET carrier One copy of met. The horse is completely normal and there is no way to see it - but half its foals inherit the copy, and two carriers bred together are the only way embryonic lethal (met) appears.
PATN1/n · PATN1/PATN1 PATN1 (leopard pattern) Natural Natural white genes American Bashkir Curly, Appaloosa, Colorado Ranger (Solid Rangerbred, Leopard Rangerbred), Kiger Mustang, Knabstrupper, Mustang, Pony of the Americas, Shetland Pony, Tarpan, Walkaloosa Wild type Carried, but silent on its own - it only shapes the pattern of a horse that also carries the leopard complex.
H/N SCN4A (HYPP) Natural Natural health genes American Bashkir Curly (rare), American Paint, Australian Stock Horse (rare), Azteca (rare), Quarter Horse (rare) HYPP (periodic paralysis) A sodium-channel defect. The muscle locks up in episodes the horse cannot control - it tires early, moves stiffly and jumps badly, and one copy is enough to cause it.
H/H SCN4A (HYPP) Natural Natural health genes no breed HYPP (two copies) Two copies of the defect. The foal cannot breathe reliably from the moment it is born and does not survive it.
P/N GYS1 (PSSM1) Natural Natural health genes American Bashkir Curly, American Paint, Australian Stock Horse, Azteca, Percheron, Quarter Horse, Shire, Vyatka PSSM1 (tying-up) The muscle stores sugar it cannot burn. The horse ties up under work - it tires sooner than it should and does not move freely afterwards.
P/P GYS1 (PSSM1) Natural Natural health genes Vyatka PSSM1 (two copies) Two copies. The same disorder, worse: episodes come on under less work and take more out of the horse. It still lives an ordinary life.
PATN2/n · PATN2/PATN2 PATN2 (blanket pattern) Natural Natural white genes American Bashkir Curly, Appaloosa, Colorado Ranger (Solid Rangerbred, Leopard Rangerbred), Kiger Mustang, Knabstrupper, Mustang, Pony of the Americas, Shetland Pony, Tarpan (Ancient leopard), Walkaloosa Wild type Carried, but silent on its own - it only shapes the pattern of a horse that also carries the leopard complex.
herda/N PPIB (HERDA) Natural Natural health genes American Paint, Australian Stock Horse (rare), Azteca (rare), Kiger Mustang, Mustang, Quarter Horse PPIB (HERDA) carrier One copy of herda. The horse is completely normal and there is no way to see it - but half its foals inherit the copy, and two carriers bred together are the only way herda (fragile skin) appears.
herda/herda PPIB (HERDA) Natural Natural health genes no breed HERDA (fragile skin) A collagen defect in the skin. It splits and scars where a saddle sits and never heals cleanly, and the horse is sore enough that it will not work properly. It survives; it is just never sound.

scid/N PRKDC (SCID) Natural Natural health genes Anglo-Arabian, Arabian PRKDC (SCID) carrier One copy of scid. The horse is completely normal and there is no way to see it - but half its foals inherit the copy, and two carriers bred together are the only way scid (no immune system) appears.
scid/scid PRKDC (SCID) Natural Natural health genes no breed SCID (no immune system) The foal is born with no working immune system at all. It is healthy for a few days on its dam's antibodies and then has nothing left to fight with.
lfs/N MYO5A (lavender foal) Natural Natural health genes Anglo-Arabian, Arabian MYO5A (lavender foal) carrier One copy of lfs. The horse is completely normal and there is no way to see it - but half its foals inherit the copy, and two carriers bred together are the only way lavender foal syndrome appears.
lfs/lfs MYO5A (lavender foal) Natural Natural health genes no breed Lavender foal syndrome A neurological defect that arrives with a diluted, silvery coat. The foal cannot stand, lies rigid with its head thrown back, and does not survive.
ca/N TOE1 (cerebellar abiotrophy) Natural Natural health genes American Bashkir Curly, Anglo-Arabian, Arabian TOE1 (cerebellar abiotrophy) carrier One copy of ca. The horse is completely normal and there is no way to see it - but half its foals inherit the copy, and two carriers bred together are the only way cerebellar abiotrophy appears.
ca/ca TOE1 (cerebellar abiotrophy) Natural Natural health genes no breed Cerebellar abiotrophy The part of the brain that governs balance wastes away after birth. The horse has no sense of where its feet are: it moves in a wide, over-reaching stagger and cannot judge a jump at all.
cvm/N CVM (cervical malformation) Natural Natural health genes no breed CVM (cervical malformation) carrier One copy of cvm. The horse is completely normal and there is no way to see it - but half its foals inherit the copy, and two carriers bred together are the only way cervical vertebral malformation appears.
cvm/cvm CVM (cervical malformation) Natural Natural health genes no breed Cervical vertebral malformation The neck vertebrae form crooked and pinch the spinal cord where it passes through them. The foal cannot coordinate its hindquarters and does not get up.
gbed/N GBE1 (GBED) Natural Natural health genes Australian Stock Horse, Azteca, Kiger Mustang, Mustang GBE1 (GBED) carrier One copy of gbed. The horse is completely normal and there is no way to see it - but half its foals inherit the copy, and two carriers bred together are the only way glycogen branching enzyme deficiency appears.
gbed/gbed GBE1 (GBED) Natural Natural health genes no breed Glycogen branching enzyme deficiency The foal cannot store or release sugar. It is weak from birth, cannot keep itself warm, and has nothing to draw on.
meg/N Megaesophagus Natural Natural health genes Friesian (rare) Megaesophagus carrier One copy of meg. The horse is completely normal and there is no way to see it - but half its foals inherit the copy, and two carriers bred together are the only way megaesophagus appears.
meg/meg Megaesophagus Natural Natural health genes no breed Megaesophagus The gullet is slack and does not push milk down. The foal chokes on what it swallows and inhales the rest.
fis/N SLC5A3 Natural Natural health genes Dales Pony, Fell Pony SLC5A3 carrier One copy of fis. The horse is completely normal and there is no way to see it - but half its foals inherit the copy, and two carriers bred together are the only way foal immunodeficiency syndrome appears.
fis/fis SLC5A3 Natural Natural health genes no breed Foal immunodeficiency syndrome No working B cells and a failing marrow. The foal is born looking perfectly healthy and fades as its dam's borrowed antibodies run out - anaemia, then an infection it cannot answer. It does not survive.
Suc/Suc Suit Magical Magical coat genes no breed Coloured suit The same split, with the caudal field in a colour rather than pale. Fully recessive: two copies, and a single copy beside the white allele shows white. It never gives blue eyes.
Suk/n · Suk/Suc · Suk/Suk Suit Magical Magical coat genes no breed Black suit The same marking as the white form with the light taken out of it: a single near-black tone.
Sut/n · Sut/Suc · Sut/Suk · Sut/Sut Suit Magical Magical coat genes no breed Suit The suit line wraps the base of the neck, its back edge landing well forward of the scapula and its front edge running up onto the neck itself - but it passes under the crest rather than over it, so the topline of the neck keeps its colour the whole way. Everything behind the band goes pale, roughly half the animal, while the head, the upper neck and the crest keep theirs. The legs come back to the coat from the knee and hock down, over a soft transition rather than a line. The mane keeps its colour; the tail follows the pale field about half the time.
Hd/Hd Hood Magical Magical coat genes no breed Hood Opaque over the whole head - muzzle, ears and all - and from there it weakens continuously back along the neck until it has vanished entirely, somewhere between the throatlatch and the shoulder depending on the horse. It runs out evenly all the way round: the crest, the sides and the throat lose it together rather than the top going first, so what travels down the neck is a band square to it and not a diagonal. There is no edge to it at any point; it simply runs out, and it starts running out well up the neck rather than near the shoulder. Both the colour and how far it carries are drawn once and passed on.
jeb/N LAMC2 Natural Natural health genes American Cream Draft LAMC2 carrier One copy of jeb. The horse is completely normal and there is no way to see it - but half its foals inherit the copy, and two carriers bred together are the only way junctional epidermolysis bullosa (lamc2) appears.
jeb/jeb LAMC2 Natural Natural health genes no breed Junctional epidermolysis bullosa (LAMC2) The layer that anchors skin to the body never formed. The hoof walls separate and the skin tears away wherever the foal puts weight. It does not survive.
jeb/N LAMA3 Natural Natural health genes American Saddlebred LAMA3 carrier One copy of jeb. The horse is completely normal and there is no way to see it - but half its foals inherit the copy, and two carriers bred together are the only way junctional epidermolysis bullosa (lama3) appears.
jeb/jeb LAMA3 Natural Natural health genes no breed Junctional epidermolysis bullosa (LAMA3) A missing piece of LAMA3, and the skin has nothing to hold on to. The hoof capsules come away and the skin splits at every pressure point. The foal does not survive.
Wm/Wm Magic white Magical Magical coat genes Dhampir (White) Magic white White from nose to tail, painted over whatever coat the horse really has - which is still in its genes for a foal to inherit. It is painted low in the magical order, so any marking the horse carries still draws on top of it.
MH/N RYR1 Natural Natural health genes Azteca (rare) Malignant hyperthermia One copy of the RYR1 variant. The horse is completely normal to look at and to ride - the fault only shows under anaesthesia or extreme stress, which is why the condition is screened for rather than spotted.
MH/MH RYR1 Natural Natural health genes no breed Malignant hyperthermia (two copies) Two copies of the RYR1 variant, and no normal calcium channel left. The horse overheats and stiffens under any real exertion, and has fewer hearts for it.
Syn/n · Syn/Syn Synort Magical Magical coat genes no breed Synort The gradient is the whole horse: it starts cool over the head, neck and upper shoulder and travels the spectrum backward along the body, with no banding and nothing repeated. Laid into it is a tobiano-shaped region that keeps the horse's real coat colour, drawn round in a black outline so it reads as cut out rather than painted on. White markings sit over or under it as they like. The mane and tail keep their natural colour and carry a sprinkle of white spots; the hooves are black; and there is no regular mane striping.
So/So Serpentine Magical Magical coat genes no breed Coloured ore The same stone, with the ore running a colour this line of horses carries instead of green.
Sp/n · Sp/So · Sp/Sp Serpentine Magical Magical coat genes no breed Serpentine Streaky bands of taupe, sage and blue-grey run the length of the coat, seamed with dark red-brown, with bright green ore scattered through them.
Cm/Cm Coastline Magical Magical coat genes no breed Coloured coastline The same map in two colours this line of horses carries, land against sea.
Cl/n · Cl/Cm · Cl/Cl Coastline Magical Magical coat genes no breed Coastline A rust landmass across a dark sage sea, outlined in pale grey, with small rust islands scattered off its shore.
Sc/Sc Squiggle Magical Magical coat genes no breed Coloured squiggle The same lanes running out from a pale middle through a colour this line of horses carries.
Sq/n · Sq/Sc · Sq/Sq Squiggle Magical Magical coat genes no breed Squiggle Wandering lanes run the length of the coat, white through their middles and fading out through yellow to a pink edge, perforated with small holes of bare coat.
Dc/Dc Dustfall Magical Magical coat genes no breed Single-colour dust The same three falls of dust, all of them in one colour this line of horses runs to, separated by how pale each one settles rather than by hue.
Du/n · Du/Dc · Du/Du Dustfall Magical Magical coat genes no breed Dustfall Chips of raspberry, yellow and teal dust lie scattered over the whole coat, each colour drifting thicker in its own places, with a faint taupe bloom under the heaviest of it.
Mnstrp/n · Mnstrp/Mnstrp Mane colour Magical Magical coat genes no breed Striped mane Bands of one bright colour run across the mane, irregularly spaced. The colour and the spacing come from the copy that carries it.
Mnsld/n · Mnsld/Mnsld Mane colour Magical Magical coat genes no breed Solid mane The whole mane is one bright colour, kept from the copy that carries it and passed on with the allele.
Mnsld/Mnstrp Mane colour Magical Magical coat genes no breed Striped over solid One copy of each. The mane takes the solid copy's colour and the striped copy's bands are drawn over it - two independent colours on one horse, which is a look neither allele can make on its own.
Bc/Bc Blackwork Magical Magical coat genes no breed Coloured blackwork The same ornament in a deep colour this line of horses runs to.
Bk/n · Bk/Bc · Bk/Bk Blackwork Magical Magical coat genes no breed Blackwork A single connected black ornament lies along the back, its curling strokes mirrored either side of the spine and tapering to points over the croup and the withers.
Tlstrp/n · Tlstrp/Tlstrp Tail colour Magical Magical coat genes no breed Striped tail Bands of one bright colour run across the tail, irregularly spaced. The colour and the spacing come from the copy that carries it.
Tlsld/n · Tlsld/Tlsld Tail colour Magical Magical coat genes no breed Solid tail The whole tail is one bright colour, kept from the copy that carries it and passed on with the allele.
Tlsld/Tlstrp Tail colour Magical Magical coat genes no breed Striped over solid One copy of each. The tail takes the solid copy's colour and the striped copy's bands are drawn over it - two independent colours on one horse, which is a look neither allele can make on its own.
Tc/Tc Teardrop Magical Magical coat genes no breed Coloured teardrop The same scatter of teardrops and the same pair of shoulder dots, in two colours this line of horses runs to.
Td/n · Td/Tc · Td/Td Teardrop Magical Magical coat genes no breed Teardrop Brilliant blue teardrops hang point-down over the coat, navy-rimmed with a cyan spark in each head, and one pink teardrop sits high on either shoulder.
Sc/Sc Shallows Magical Magical coat genes no breed Coloured shallows The same water and the same splotches in two colours this line of horses runs to, which need not agree with each other.
Sh/n · Sh/Sc · Sh/Sh Shallows Magical Magical coat genes no breed Shallows A pale wet ground carrying rings of short blue flecks that spread from a centre on each part, lime splotches scattered over them, and a black depth reaching down from the back in lobed fingers.
Hlr/Hlr Healer Magical Magical coat genes no breed Healer A red stripe runs down the centre of the mane, faint on some horses and vivid on others, and everyone standing within 3 blocks of the horse steadily mends.
Cc/Cc Overcast Magical Magical coat genes no breed Coloured sky The same cloud, lit from somewhere else - the banks carry a colour this line of horses runs to, still shading from a washed-out rim down to a deep core.
Ck/n · Ck/Cc · Ck/Ck Overcast Magical Magical coat genes no breed Black sky The same marking as the white form with the light taken out of it: the same 4 steps, moved to the dark end of the scale.
Cu/n · Cu/Cc · Cu/Ck · Cu/Cu Overcast Magical Magical coat genes no breed Overcast Banks of grey cloud lie across the coat in packed rounded blobs, each one shaded darker through its middle, with thin channels of bright sky running between them.
Rc/Rc Ringwork Magical Magical coat genes no breed Coloured ringwork The same network and the same nested rings, carrying a colour this line of horses runs to instead of the greys.
Rw/n · Rw/Rc · Rw/Rw Ringwork Magical Magical coat genes no breed Ringwork A near-black network runs across the coat, thickening where three lines meet, and each space it closes off holds a pale ground with two concentric rings and a dark dot at the middle.
Wf/Wf Nymphaline Magical Magical coat genes no breed Frosted wing The same venation with the pigment gone out of it - a bone-white membrane veined in grey-green, like a wing held up to the light.
Wn/n · Wn/Wf · Wn/Wn Nymphaline Magical Magical coat genes no breed Nymphaline The flank is a butterfly wing: rose membrane cut into cells by dark green veins that radiate from a heavy spar along the back, every vein edged in cream, the venation breaking down into a fine dark net over the lower hindquarter.
Cq/Cq Crazework Magical Magical coat genes no breed Coloured crazework The same tiling in two colours this line of horses runs to - one carrying the plates and their cores, the other the ground and the junctions.
Cw/n · Cw/Cq · Cw/Cw Crazework Magical Magical coat genes no breed Crazework Dark tiled plates with blue centres and cyan sparks, set in grey channels that pool gold at every junction, breaking apart into islands toward the front of the horse.
Mzeb/n Magic zebra Magical Magical coat genes no breed Dusky-banded One copy darkens the zebra map instead of blacking it, so the horse wears the full pattern as dusky bands a few shades down from its own colour. Obvious on a pale coat, subtle on a dark one - and visible enough to breed from, which is the point of it.
Mzeb/Mzeb Magic zebra Magical Magical coat genes no breed Magic zebra A zebra's own stripe map painted in hard black: vertical bands off the spine, arcs round the hip, rings down the legs, narrow bands on the neck and face, a black dorsal stripe and muzzle, and an unstriped belly. Per-horse in spacing, width, bend and leg reach. They read black over any coat at all, including a cremello or a dominant white.
Wc/Wc Dorsal Wing Magical Magical coat genes no breed Coloured dorsal wing The same butterfly in two colours this line of horses runs to - one across the wing's markings, the other along its margin.
Wb/n · Wb/Wc · Wb/Wb Dorsal Wing Magical Magical coat genes no breed Dorsal Wing A dark butterfly lies along the back with its wings spread either side of the spine, edged in teal, carrying rust squiggles with hot pink cells inside them - the same on both halves.
Glo/Glo Right glowing iris Magical Magical coat genes no breed Right glowing iris This iris renders at full brightness, in whatever colour the iris already is. An invisible iris has nothing to light, and shows no glow.
Cx/Cx Contour Magical Magical coat genes no breed Coloured contour The same map in one colour this line of horses runs to, the levels separated by lightness alone.
Ct/n · Ct/Cx · Ct/Ct Contour Magical Magical coat genes no breed Contour Lobed masses ringed by nested bands stepping from cream through pink and mint down to near-black, with a hard dark line tracing one level.
Glo/Glo Left glowing iris Magical Magical coat genes no breed Left glowing iris This iris renders at full brightness, in whatever colour the iris already is. An invisible iris has nothing to light, and shows no glow.
Glo/Glo Right glowing sclera Magical Magical coat genes Dhampir Right glowing sclera The white of this eye renders at full brightness, in whatever colour the sclera already is - a lit ring with the iris a hole in the middle of it.
Dx/Dx Dripwork Magical Magical coat genes no breed Coloured dripwork The same rings and drips in an ink this line of horses carries.
Dw/n · Dw/Dx · Dw/Dw Dripwork Magical Magical coat genes no breed Dripwork Black rings with solid centres sit on a near-white ground, each trailing a drip downward, with smaller solid dots scattered between them.
Glo/Glo Left glowing sclera Magical Magical coat genes Dhampir Left glowing sclera The white of this eye renders at full brightness, in whatever colour the sclera already is - a lit ring with the iris a hole in the middle of it.
Def/Def Third eye Magical Magical coat genes no breed Third eye (defined) An eye that owes nothing to the other two: every part of it - iris colour, sector, sector colour, sclera, and whether either half glows - is written on the allele copy, drawn from the same palettes the real eye loci offer.
Mix/Mix Third eye Magical Magical coat genes no breed Third eye (mixed) An eye assembled from both of the others - iris from one side, sclera from the other, and so on for each part independently. Which side each part came from is written on the allele copy, so it is inherited and it drifts.
Rgt/Rgt Third eye Magical Magical coat genes no breed Third eye (right imitation) The same, copied from the right eye instead - which is a different horse entirely if the two eyes differ.
Lft/Lft Third eye Magical Magical coat genes no breed Third eye (left imitation) An eye in the middle of the forehead, identical to the horse's left eye in every respect - colour, sector, sclera and glow.
n/n Milk Magical Yield and death no breed Mare's milk The ordinary horse. A grown mare can be milked into a bucket; nothing about the coat changes.
Lava/n · Watr/n Milk Magical Yield and death no breed Milk carrier One magical copy, which shows nothing - the horse still gives plain milk, and only if it is a mare. Two carriers bred together are the only way to see the rest of this locus.
Lava/Lava Milk Magical Yield and death no breed Lava-bearing Two lava copies. Any grown horse of either sex fills a bucket with lava, slowly. The horse is not harmed by it and does not set anything alight.
Watr/Watr Milk Magical Yield and death no breed Water-bearing Two water copies. Any grown horse of either sex fills a bucket with water, indefinitely and anywhere.
Mlk/n Magic milk volume Magical Yield and death no breed Milky One milky copy. The horse can be filled from once plus the number written on that copy - usually one to three more times - before it has to wait out the day. Unlike most of the magical loci a single copy shows, so a milky horse can be found rather than only bred.
Mlk/Mlk Magic milk volume Magical Yield and death no breed Very milky Two milky copies, and the numbers add. Four to seven fillings a day from an average pair, and more from a line selected for it - the numbers drift a little every generation, so a dairy line keeps improving on the horses it started from.
Mty/Mty Magic meat Magical Yield and death no breed Meaty Two meaty copies. The horse drops meat as well as everything else it would have dropped. How much is a number written on the copies rather than on the allele - averaged between the two, inherited with them, and drifting a little every generation - so a line bred for it yields more than the horses it started from.
Swd/Swd Magic item drop Magical Yield and death no breed Sword-bearing Two sword copies. The horse drops an enchanted sword, with one enchantment rolled when it dies rather than fixed by the gene - so two horses of identical genotype do not leave identical swords, and this is the one drop here that is a gamble rather than a certainty.
Egg/Egg Magic item drop Magical Yield and death no breed Self-seeding Two self-seeding copies. The horse drops a spawn egg carrying its own genotype AND its own epigenome, so what comes back out is a clone - the same coat, the same colours, the same percentages written on every allele copy - and not merely a horse with the same alleles. It is the only thing in the mod that undoes a death.
Dia/Dia Magic item drop Magical Yield and death no breed Diamond-bearing Two diamond copies. The horse drops 2 to 5 diamonds instead of its leather. There is nothing about the living animal that shows this, which is the point: it is worth breeding a line for and impossible to spot in a field.
Xpl/Xpl Magic on death Magical Yield and death no breed Volatile Two volatile copies. The horse goes off like a creeper when it dies, with everything that implies for whatever killed it, whatever was standing nearby, and the ground underneath. No wild horse carries two, so every one of these was bred on purpose by somebody.
Wat/Wat Magic on death Magical Yield and death no breed Water spring Two water copies. A water source appears where the horse died - the useful half of the same idea, and by some distance the safest thing on this locus.
Lav/Lav Magic on death Magical Yield and death no breed Lava spring Two lava copies. When the horse dies a lava source appears where it was standing. It is a real source block and it flows, so where the horse dies matters rather a lot.
Bai/Bai Magic mob aura Magical Behaviour no breed Baiting Two baiting copies. Any hostile mob within about 16 blocks that can see the horse picks it as its target over anything else in range, including its rider. Whether that is a shield or a very short career depends entirely on what else the horse was bred with.
Wrd/Wrd Magic mob aura Magical Behaviour no breed Warding Two warding copies. Mobs will not approach within about 10 blocks - they turn away at the edge rather than being hurt or teleported. A horse worth keeping a stable around.
Aaa/Aaa Aggression Magical Behaviour no breed Hunts everything always Two copies. At any hour the horse goes for anything alive that comes within reach, its own owner included within about 16 blocks - and it holds its ground rather than chasing. Riding it is no protection: it throws you out of the saddle and comes for you.

Wbh/Wbh Magic night watch Magical Behaviour no breed Stands directly behind you Two copies. It gets as close behind you as it can and stays there - but only while you are not looking at it. Turn round and it is an ordinary horse again, which is the whole point of it. It is the last step of the progression and the one people ask for.
Wun/Wun Magic night watch Magical Behaviour no breed Watches from where you cannot see it Two copies. It works out where you are looking and puts itself somewhere else - behind you, round a corner, out of the arc - and watches from there. Turn round and it is already moving. It makes no sound doing any of it.
Wsi/Wsi Magic night watch Magical Behaviour no breed Watches while it can see you Two copies. It watches only while it genuinely has line of sight, and goes back to grazing the moment you step behind something. The gentlest of the five, and the only one that can be escaped by closing a door.
Wnr/Wnr Magic night watch Magical Behaviour no breed Closes, then stares Two copies. The same stare, but it comes to about 10 blocks first and then stops. It does not come closer and it does not go away, and it makes no sound arriving.
Wst/Wst Magic night watch Magical Behaviour no breed Stares without moving Two copies. After dark the horse stops. It does not graze, it does not wander, it does not follow - it turns to face the nearest player and holds there, through walls, through floors, from across the valley. It makes no sound while it does it. At dawn it goes back to being a horse.
Sun/n Sun sensitivity Magical Behaviour Dhampir (Seal brown) Sun sensitivity carrier One copy, which shows nothing: the horse stands in the sun like any other. Two carriers bred together is the only way a sun-sensitive horse appears.
Sun/Sun Sun sensitivity Magical Behaviour Dhampir (White) Sun-sensitive Burned by daylight. Under open sky by day it takes half a heart every two seconds and runs for the nearest deep cover or water, jumping a fence to get there, and it stays under cover until nightfall. Rain, water and a roof all keep it safe.
Small/n Magic body size Magical Magical body-stat genes set by stat scores Smaller One small copy. The same thing in reverse: smaller by the percentage on that copy, usually around a tenth, and inherited with the allele.
Small/Small Magic body size Magical Magical body-stat genes set by stat scores Much smaller Two small copies, and the percentages subtract together. Around a fifth under on average and a great deal less at the extreme, which is where the very smallest horses come from.
Big/n Magic body size Magical Magical body-stat genes set by stat scores Larger One big copy. The horse is larger than its own genes would make it, by the percentage written on that copy - usually around a tenth. It passes that exact percentage on with the allele.
Big/Small Magic body size Magical Magical body-stat genes set by stat scores Balanced One big copy and one small copy. Their percentages very nearly cancel, so the horse is close to its ordinary size while carrying, and passing on, both extremes. A horse a hair off normal is usually this.
Big/Big Magic body size Magical Magical body-stat genes set by stat scores Much larger Two big copies, and the percentages add. Around a fifth over on average, and far more when both copies rolled well - the largest horses in the world are bred here, because no wild horse is born with two.

Gil/n Magic water breathing Magical Magical body-stat genes no breed Longer breath One gilled copy. The horse lasts longer under water before the first drowning tick, by the percentage written on that copy - usually about a quarter. It swims no faster; this is lung capacity, not propulsion.

Wmp/n Magic fighter Magical Magical body-stat genes no breed Weak One wimp copy. The horse hits for less than the baseline by the percentage on that copy - and carries it silently into every cross.
Wmp/Wmp Magic fighter Magical Magical body-stat genes no breed Harmless Two wimp copies, subtracting together. The horse hits for very little, and never for less than 1 point however far the percentages go: a horse that could not hurt anything at all would be a different animal, not a weaker one.
Gld/n Magic fighter Magical Magical body-stat genes no breed Fighter One gladiator copy. The horse hits harder than the baseline by the percentage written on that copy, and passes that exact percentage on with the allele. A wild one is worth between one and three extra points.
Gld/Wmp Magic fighter Magical Magical body-stat genes no breed Balanced One gladiator copy and one wimp copy. They are added with opposite signs, so the wimp counts against the gladiator and the horse comes out near the baseline - while passing both on. This is why breeding a fighter means breeding the wimp allele out as much as breeding the gladiator in.
Gld/Gld Magic fighter Magical Magical body-stat genes no breed Champion Two gladiator copies, and the percentages add. There is no ceiling on this and no wild horse is born with two, so every genuinely dangerous horse in a world is one somebody bred - and a line selected for it keeps climbing, because the percentage drifts a little with each generation.

Wlf/Wlf Lycanthropy Magical Behaviour no breed Wolf At nightfall this horse becomes a wolf - a real one, which can be done everything to that a wolf can be done to, and nothing else. It cannot be ridden, it trails a faint cloud of coloured dust as it moves, and anything that strikes it is followed until sunrise. At dawn it is the same horse it was, with the same name and the same pedigree.

· Aly/Arma Lycanthropy Magical Behaviour no breed Two shapes, neither taken Two different shapes, one on each copy, and the horse stays a horse - a shift needs the same animal twice. Both are carried and both are passed on, so this horse is one parent of two different lines.
Wtr/Wtr Lycanthropy Magical Behaviour no breed Wandering trader At nightfall this horse becomes a wandering trader - a real one, which can be done everything to that a wandering trader can be done to, and nothing else. It cannot be ridden, it trails a faint cloud of coloured dust as it moves, and anything that strikes it is followed until sunrise. At dawn it is the same horse it was, with the same name and the same pedigree.
