C/C Wing Margin Magical Magical coat genes no breed Coloured margin The wing takes the line's own colour, dark-veined with a banded, scalloped margin.
B/n · B/C · B/B Wing Margin Magical Magical coat genes no breed Mourning margin The wing in mourning colours - a slate field, black veins and near-black lunules.
W/n · W/C · W/B · W/W Wing Margin Magical Magical coat genes no breed Chalk margin A white wing field over the flank, veined in grey and scalloped along the underline.
MltM/n · MltC/n · MltC/MltM · MltB/n · MltB/MltM · MltB/MltC Molten hooves Magical Trails and emissions no breed Carries molten hooves No prints. This horse carries a recessive molten allele - black, colour or multicolour - that only shows in a horse with two copies of the same one.
MltM/MltM Molten hooves Magical Trails and emissions no breed Molten hooves: multicolour Glowing hoofprints that run through several colours - each copy's two, one print after another - all of them written on the alleles. Two copies of the multicolour allele.
MltC/MltC Molten hooves Magical Trails and emissions no breed Molten hooves: colour Glowing hoofprints in one colour, written on the allele and inherited with it, so a line breeds true to its own fire. Two copies of the colour allele.
MltB/MltB Molten hooves Magical Trails and emissions no breed Molten hooves: black Black hoofprints that do not glow - a scorch rather than an ember. Two copies of the black allele; a single one shows nothing.
MltW/n · MltW/MltM · MltW/MltC · MltW/MltB · MltW/MltW Molten hooves Magical Trails and emissions no breed Molten hooves: white Glowing white hoofprints on the ground behind the horse, fading as they cool. White is dominant: one copy is all of it, whatever the other copy is. Nothing catches fire.
Frp/Frp Fireproof Magical Behaviour no breed Fireproof Two copies. Neither the horse nor its rider takes fire damage, and the horse swims through lava the way an ordinary horse swims through water - slowly, at the surface, entirely unbothered. A lava lake stops being a detour.
Brd/Brd Bird boned Magical Behaviour no breed Bird-boned Two copies. Neither the horse nor its rider takes any falling damage at all, from any height. Mountains stop having a wrong side and a ravine becomes a shortcut.
Ocn/Ocn Ocean-born Magical Behaviour no breed Ocean-born Two copies. Neither the horse nor its rider ever drowns, however long you stay under. It says nothing about how fast the horse swims or how it behaves in water - those are other loci, on purpose.
Hyd/Hyd Hydrophobic Magical Behaviour no breed Hydrophobic Two copies. In water deep enough to swim in, the horse throws its rider and heads for land. It is exactly vanilla horse behaviour, which is what makes it a fault rather than a feature in a mod that fixed it.
Hot/Hot Hot blooded Magical Behaviour no breed Hot-blooded Two copies. Snow and ice within a couple of blocks melt away, slowly and continuously, leaving a thawed circle wherever the horse stands. It does not make the horse immune to anything cold - it only removes the cold thing.
Lteye/n · Lteye/Lteye Light Magical Magical coat genes no breed Glowing eyes The eyes are gold and burn bright in the dark - by far the smallest of the three, and the one that carries furthest at night. The horse itself gives off light at level 14, the same as a torch.
Ltmn/n · Ltmn/Ltmn Light Magical Magical coat genes no breed Glowing mane The mane is gold and burns bright in the dark. A foal has no mane yet, so it lights its surroundings without anything visibly glowing. The horse itself gives off light at level 14, the same as a torch.
Ltmn/Lteye Light Magical Magical coat genes no breed Glowing mane and eyes One copy of each. Both show, in full. The horse itself gives off light at level 14, the same as a torch.
Lthf/n · Lthf/Lthf Light Magical Magical coat genes no breed Glowing hooves All four hooves are gold and burn bright in the dark. The horse itself gives off light at level 14, the same as a torch.
Lthf/Lteye Light Magical Magical coat genes no breed Glowing hooves and eyes One copy of each. Both show, in full. The horse itself gives off light at level 14, the same as a torch.
Lthf/Ltmn Light Magical Magical coat genes no breed Glowing hooves and mane One copy of each. Both show, in full - the alleles do not compete. The horse itself gives off light at level 14, the same as a torch.
Bone/n · Flwr/n · Mush/n · Dark/n · Aca/n · Jung/n · Spru/n · Brch/n · Oak/n Dryad Magical Behaviour no breed Dryad carrier One variant copy and one wild type. Nothing happens - a variant is still recessive to the wild type - and the horse passes it on. Two variants is a different matter, even when they are different variants.
Bone/Bone Dryad Magical Behaviour no breed Bone meal dryad Two matching copies. Every so often the horse leaves a hurried-along growing thing near where it is standing - it fertilises instead of planting - saplings, grass, flowers, moss and mushrooms, and pointedly never a crop. How often is written on the allele copy, so breeding can hurry it. This is the only way to get one thing reliably.
Flwr/Bone Dryad Magical Behaviour no breed Flower and bone meal Two different variants, and <b>both</b> express - each at half its usual rate, so the horse leaves things as often as a matched pair does and alternates between flowers and a hurried-along growing thing. It will never make a dark oak grove and it will never make a monoculture either.
Flwr/Flwr Dryad Magical Behaviour no breed Flower dryad Two matching copies. Every so often the horse leaves flowers near where it is standing - whatever the biome would have grown there anyway, one at a time. How often is written on the allele copy, so breeding can hurry it. This is the only way to get one thing reliably.
Mush/Bone Dryad Magical Behaviour no breed Mushroom and bone meal Two different variants, and <b>both</b> express - each at half its usual rate, so the horse leaves things as often as a matched pair does and alternates between mushrooms and a hurried-along growing thing. It will never make a dark oak grove and it will never make a monoculture either.
Mush/Flwr Dryad Magical Behaviour no breed Mushroom and flower Two different variants, and <b>both</b> express - each at half its usual rate, so the horse leaves things as often as a matched pair does and alternates between mushrooms and flowers. It will never make a dark oak grove and it will never make a monoculture either.
Mush/Mush Dryad Magical Behaviour no breed Mushroom dryad Two matching copies. Every so often the horse leaves mushrooms near where it is standing - red and brown, and only where they will survive: out of the light, or on mycelium and podzol where the light does not matter. How often is written on the allele copy, so breeding can hurry it. This is the only way to get one thing reliably.
Dark/Bone Dryad Magical Behaviour no breed Dark oak and bone meal Two different variants, and <b>both</b> express - each at half its usual rate, so the horse leaves things as often as a matched pair does and alternates between dark oak saplings and a hurried-along growing thing. It will never make a dark oak grove and it will never make a monoculture either.
Dark/Flwr Dryad Magical Behaviour no breed Dark oak and flower Two different variants, and <b>both</b> express - each at half its usual rate, so the horse leaves things as often as a matched pair does and alternates between dark oak saplings and flowers. It will never make a dark oak grove and it will never make a monoculture either.
Dark/Mush Dryad Magical Behaviour no breed Dark oak and mushroom Two different variants, and <b>both</b> express - each at half its usual rate, so the horse leaves things as often as a matched pair does and alternates between dark oak saplings and mushrooms. It will never make a dark oak grove and it will never make a monoculture either.
Dark/Dark Dryad Magical Behaviour no breed Dark oak dryad Two matching copies. Every so often the horse leaves dark oak saplings near where it is standing - the only one that needs four of itself in a square - so it plants beside what it has already planted, and a grove takes a few days rather than one. How often is written on the allele copy, so breeding can hurry it. This is the only way to get one thing reliably.
Aca/Bone Dryad Magical Behaviour no breed Acacia and bone meal Two different variants, and <b>both</b> express - each at half its usual rate, so the horse leaves things as often as a matched pair does and alternates between acacia saplings and a hurried-along growing thing. It will never make a dark oak grove and it will never make a monoculture either.
Aca/Flwr Dryad Magical Behaviour no breed Acacia and flower Two different variants, and <b>both</b> express - each at half its usual rate, so the horse leaves things as often as a matched pair does and alternates between acacia saplings and flowers. It will never make a dark oak grove and it will never make a monoculture either.
Aca/Mush Dryad Magical Behaviour no breed Acacia and mushroom Two different variants, and <b>both</b> express - each at half its usual rate, so the horse leaves things as often as a matched pair does and alternates between acacia saplings and mushrooms. It will never make a dark oak grove and it will never make a monoculture either.
Aca/Dark Dryad Magical Behaviour no breed Acacia and dark oak Two different variants, and <b>both</b> express - each at half its usual rate, so the horse leaves things as often as a matched pair does and alternates between acacia saplings and dark oak saplings. It will never make a dark oak grove and it will never make a monoculture either.
Aca/Aca Dryad Magical Behaviour no breed Acacia dryad Two matching copies. Every so often the horse leaves acacia saplings near where it is standing - grows crooked, so an acacia line never makes the same shape twice. How often is written on the allele copy, so breeding can hurry it. This is the only way to get one thing reliably.
Jung/Bone Dryad Magical Behaviour no breed Jungle and bone meal Two different variants, and <b>both</b> express - each at half its usual rate, so the horse leaves things as often as a matched pair does and alternates between jungle saplings and a hurried-along growing thing. It will never make a dark oak grove and it will never make a monoculture either.
Jung/Flwr Dryad Magical Behaviour no breed Jungle and flower Two different variants, and <b>both</b> express - each at half its usual rate, so the horse leaves things as often as a matched pair does and alternates between jungle saplings and flowers. It will never make a dark oak grove and it will never make a monoculture either.

It will never make a dark oak grove and it will never make a monoculture either.

Oak/Oak Dryad Magical Behaviour no breed Oak dryad Two matching copies. Every so often the horse leaves oak saplings near where it is standing - the common one, and the fastest to become a tree. Itm/Itm Intimidating Magical Behaviour no breed Intimidating Two copies. Everything that is not a horse - your cows and sheep as readily as any monster - is pushed out of a radius written on the allele copy, at least eight blocks. It is the quietest place in the world to stand, and an intimidating horse cannot be kept in a farm without emptying it.
Mew/Mew Meowing Magical Behaviour no breed Meowing Two copies. The horse meows now and then - not often, not loudly, and with no explanation available anywhere in its genome - and creepers stay outside a radius written on the allele copy.
Svc/Svc Stormvein Magical Magical coat genes no breed Coloured Stormvein The same marbling in two colours the line carries, one for the field and one for the veins. Fully recessive.
Svn/n · Svn/Svc · Svn/Svn Stormvein Magical Magical coat genes no breed Stormvein Electric violet marbling over the whole horse, rimmed dark around pools of bare coat and veined with forking cyan that brightens to white at its core.
Cln/Cln Cleansing light Magical Behaviour no breed Cleansing light Two copies. Zombies, skeletons, drowned and anything else that counts as undead take steady damage while they stand within a radius written on the allele copy. Anything the aura kills was killed by the horse, so you get no experience and none of the drops that only fall to a player - it is a defensive gene, not a farming one.
Hly/Hly Holy ward Magical Behaviour no breed Warding Two copies. Hostile mobs cannot spawn within a radius written on the allele copy - at least eight blocks and never more than sixteen. Anywhere the horse is standing is somewhere nothing appears, which makes it a camp that walks: a mining stop, an overnight halt, a half-built base with no lighting yet.
Ech/Ech Echolocate Magical Behaviour no breed Echoing Two copies. Every so often the horse makes a bat’s cry, and everything living within a radius written on the allele copy is outlined - through walls, through the dark. It shows you the skeleton round the corner and the cow you lost, without distinguishing between them.
Alm/n · Alm/Alm Base alarm Magical Behaviour no breed Alarm One copy is enough. When something hostile comes within about sixteen blocks of the horse, it whinnies. It does nothing else about it - this announces the danger and leaves it entirely to you.
Msc/Msc Music enjoyer Magical Behaviour no breed Music-loving Two copies. While a jukebox is playing within a few blocks, the horse stands there giving off hearts and its bond rises. It still answers to the same daily bond ceiling as everything else, so this is a pleasant way to spend a day rather than a shortcut past one.
Tf/Tf Flying Magical Behaviour no breed True flight Rises, hovers and flies wherever its rider points it, indefinitely and without wings. Hold jump to climb; look where you want to go and ride forward. It does not fall, it does not stall, and nothing about the horse's body explains any of it. It takes two copies: a single one is carried and never shows.
El/n · El/Tf · El/El Flying Magical Behaviour no breed Gliding flight Glides on momentum like an elytra. A charged jump launches it; from there height is bought with speed and speed with height. It cannot hover, and a horse that runs out of both falls.
gold/chaos Magic sectoral heterochromia Magical Magical coat genes no breed Gold & chaos A randomly shaped wedge of gold and the rest of the iris chaos, with a different shape in each eye and the two colours leading opposite sides. The shapes are inherited with the alleles that drew them, and the chaos half is a colour of its own on almost every horse.

Grd/Grd Guardian Magical Behaviour no breed Guardian Two copies, and the horse must be tamed. Whatever damages its owner within about sixteen blocks gets attacked - only that, and only after the fact. Unlike the aggression locus it never picks the fight: it starts nothing, and it answers for you rather than for itself.
End/End Ender echo Magical Behaviour no breed Echoing Two copies. When something damages the horse it blinks about eight blocks away, carrying whoever is riding it. Against skeletons this is close to immunity; against a skeleton on the far side of a ravine it is a short trip you did not plan.
Day/Day Eyesight locus Magical Magical body-stat genes no breed Daywalker Two daywalker copies. Faster in good light and slower in the dark - the plainly useful one for anybody who travels by day, and a liability in a cave.
Cav/Day Eyesight locus Magical Magical body-stat genes no breed Balanced One of each. They are opposite modifiers under opposite conditions, so exactly one is ever active and across a day they cancel. The horse is ordinary and throws both.
Cav/Cav Eyesight locus Magical Magical body-stat genes no breed Caveborn Two caveborn copies. The horse moves noticeably better in the dark - underground at noon counts, because this reads the actual light and not the clock - and worse in bright light. Lighting your own tunnel slows it down, which is a real trade in a game where lighting a tunnel is also how you stop things spawning in it.
Sv/n · Sv/Sv Weather sensitive (speed) Magical Magical body-stat genes no breed Worse in snowfall While snowfall holds, the horse's moving speed falls by the percentage written on that copy. Two copies of the same allele add, and the horse is unchanged in any other weather. It is the only kind of locus in the mod you cannot check on demand - you wait for the sky.
S+/n · S+/S+ Weather sensitive (speed) Magical Magical body-stat genes no breed Better in snowfall While snowfall holds, the horse's moving speed rises by the percentage written on that copy. Two copies of the same allele add, and the horse is unchanged in any other weather. It is the only kind of locus in the mod you cannot check on demand - you wait for the sky.
S+/Sv · Tv/Sv · Tv/S+ · T+/Sv · T+/S+ · T+/Tv · Rv/Sv · Rv/S+ · Rv/Tv · Rv/T+ · R+/Sv · R+/S+ · R+/Tv · R+/T+ · R+/Rv Weather sensitive (speed) Magical Magical body-stat genes no breed Mixed weather sense Two different weather alleles. Both apply, and they never fight - each is gated on its own weather, and two kinds of weather do not both hold. A horse that is better in rain and worse in a storm is exactly that.

Fed cocoa beans, this horse gets a short burst of speed and a great deal more bond than the food deserves - and it will accept them even if its diet says otherwise, which is the point of the locus rather than an oversight.

Hst/n Potion milk Magical Yield and death no breed Haste milk One copy. Milking her with an empty bottle gets a weak potion of Haste.
Hst/Hst Potion milk Magical Yield and death no breed Strong Haste milk Two matching copies, and the locus is incompletely dominant - so this is the same potion of Haste at twice the duration and a step up in strength. The clearest reason to double a brew line.

Res/Hst · Inv/Hst · Inv/Res · Jmp/Hst · Jmp/Res · Jmp/Inv · Wtr/Hst · Wtr/Res · Wtr/Inv · Wtr/Jmp · Nvs/Hst · Nvs/Res · Nvs/Inv · Nvs/Jmp · Nvs/Wtr · Fir/Hst · Fir/Res · Fir/Inv · Fir/Jmp · Fir/Wtr · Fir/Nvs · Rgn/Hst · Rgn/Res · Rgn/Inv · Rgn/Jmp · Rgn/Wtr · Rgn/Nvs · Rgn/Fir · Str/Hst · Str/Res · Str/Inv · Str/Jmp · Str/Wtr · Str/Nvs · Str/Fir · Str/Rgn · Spd/Hst · Spd/Res · Spd/Inv · Spd/Jmp · Spd/Wtr · Spd/Nvs · Spd/Fir · Spd/Rgn · Spd/Str Potion milk Magical Yield and death no breed Mixed brew Two different effects. One bottle comes back carrying BOTH of them, each at the weaker grade - not one of the two, and not nothing. A potion is not a shape, so there is no reason a horse cannot mean two things at once.

grass/n · moss/n · moss/grass · mush/n · mush/grass · mush/moss Verdant Magical Behaviour no breed Verdant carrier One variant copy, or one of two different ones. Nothing happens: every variant here needs two of itself, so a mycelium copy beside a moss copy is inert and the horse passes both on.
grass/grass Verdant Magical Behaviour no breed Grass-bearing Two grass copies. Bare dirt greens over wherever the horse stands - the gentlest of the three, and the only one that undoes damage rather than doing it.
moss/moss Verdant Magical Behaviour no breed Moss-bearing Two moss copies. Moss creeps outward from wherever the horse stands, over stone and dirt alike.
mush/mush Verdant Magical Behaviour no breed Mycelium-bearing Two mycelium copies. Mycelium creeps outward from wherever the horse stands, taking over dirt and grass, and the mushrooms follow.

Wlf/Wlf Leader of the pack Magical Behaviour no breed Leads wolfs that are already nearby Two matching copies. Every wolf within sixteen blocks trails the horse wherever it goes, and gives up when it gets too far. IT DOES NOT CREATE THEM: take this horse somewhere with no wolfs and nothing happens, which is the single most likely reason to think the gene is broken when it is working. It is a retinue rather than a command - they are not tamed, not owned, and will still wander off if something more interesting happens.

The horse settles on neither and nothing follows it, which is most crosses between two carriers and is the reason the locus is a search rather than a slot machine.

Every wandering trader within sixteen blocks trails the horse wherever it goes, and gives up when it gets too far. IT DOES NOT CREATE THEM: take this horse somewhere with no wandering traders and nothing happens, which is the single most likely reason to think the gene is broken when it is working. It is a retinue rather than a command - they are not tamed, not owned, and will still wander off if something more interesting happens.

Zvil/Zvil Spawner Magical Behaviour no breed Spawns zombie villagers Two matching copies. Feed the horse anything it eats and two zombie villagers appear nearby - every meal, as many times as you care to feed it.

How much, and whether it goes to speed, jump or some of each, is written on each copy.

Bli/Bli Blight Magical Magical coat genes no breed Blight Ground cover dies back from the hooves. Grass blocks, moss, mycelium and podzol all revert to plain dirt, and wild growth standing on them - grass, ferns and flowers - withers away. Nothing a player planted or placed is touched.
Grnpnk/Grnpnk LUT Magical Magical coat genes no breed Green-pink palette The warm red/orange/brown gradient is replaced with green through pink, so a horse that would have been black comes out green and one that would have been chestnut comes out pink. Requires two Grnpnk copies.
Blupnk/Blupnk LUT Magical Magical coat genes no breed Blue-pink palette The warm red/orange/brown gradient is replaced with a blue and pink one, so every melanin gene resolves to a dreamier, cooler colour. Requires two Blupnk copies.
Cutmrk/n Cutie mark Magical Magical coat genes no breed Cutie-mark carrier One copy, which shows nothing. Two carriers bred together are the only way a cutie mark appears (or, rarely, a wild horse born with two).
Cutmrk/Cutmrk Cutie mark Magical Magical coat genes no breed Cutie mark A small emblem of one to three items on both flanks, drawn on top of every other coat gene. Which items, how many, whether they sit in a row or a triangle, and how big they are, are all epigenetic and inherited with the allele - so a mark you like breeds true.
ATm/n · ATm/ATm Passification Magical Behaviour no breed Temporarily passified as an adult One copy is enough. Feed this horse what it asks for, only once it is grown, and it leaves you alone for a while - then wants asking again. What it wants, and how much, is written on the allele copy and inherited with it.
FTm/n · FTm/FTm Passification Magical Behaviour no breed Temporarily passified as a foal One copy is enough. Feed this horse what it asks for, only while it is a foal, and it leaves you alone for a while - then wants asking again. What it wants, and how much, is written on the allele copy and inherited with it.
FTm/ATm · APr/ATm · APr/FTm · FPr/ATm · FPr/FTm · FPr/APr · Tmp/ATm · Tmp/FTm · Tmp/APr · Tmp/FPr · Prm/ATm · Prm/FTm · Prm/APr · Prm/FPr · Prm/Tmp Passification Magical Behaviour no breed Two ways in Two different passification alleles, and the horse offers BOTH - either route works, each with its own offering. Worth more than either parent, which is the point of the locus.
APr/n · APr/APr Passification Magical Behaviour no breed Permanently passified as an adult One copy is enough. Feed this horse what it asks for, only once it is grown, and it will never target you again. What it wants, and how much, is written on the allele copy and inherited with it.
FPr/n · FPr/FPr Passification Magical Behaviour no breed Permanently passified as a foal One copy is enough. Feed this horse what it asks for, only while it is a foal, and it will never target you again. What it wants, and how much, is written on the allele copy and inherited with it.
Tmp/n · Tmp/Tmp Passification Magical Behaviour no breed Temporarily passified One copy is enough. Feed this horse what it asks for, at any age, and it leaves you alone for a while - then wants asking again. What it wants, and how much, is written on the allele copy and inherited with it.
Prm/n · Prm/Prm Passification Magical Behaviour no breed Permanently passified One copy is enough. Feed this horse what it asks for, at any age, and it will never target you again. What it wants, and how much, is written on the allele copy and inherited with it.
Quc/Quc Qular Magical Ground and strong white no breed Coloured Qular The same panelling, with the ground in a colour the line carries rather than in white. Fully recessive: it takes two copies, and a single copy beside the white allele shows white. It never gives blue eyes, whatever the white form does.
Quk/n · Quk/Quc · Quk/Quk Qular Magical Ground and strong white no breed Black Qular The same marking as the white form with the light taken out of it: a single near-black tone.
Qul/n · Qul/Quc · Qul/Quk · Qul/Qul Qular Magical Ground and strong white no breed Qular Nearly the whole horse goes white, and what is left of the coat reads as panels laid on top of it rather than as gaps cut out - big irregular polygons with straight sides and corners that meet three at a time, scattered over the neck, shoulder, ribcage, barrel, hindquarter and upper legs. Between them runs an even white channel a finger or two wide, which is what makes it a giraffe rather than a paint. The head is mostly white with panels round the eyes and muzzle; the lower legs keep a dark boot. Every panel edge is crisp - how big the panels are and how wide the channels between them run varies a great deal from horse to horse, but none of them feathers. The mane and tail are not touched by any of it.
Fdc/Fdc Fracture Magical Ground and strong white no breed Coloured Fracture The same fragmentation, with the ground in a colour the line carries rather than in white. Fully recessive: it takes two copies, and a single copy beside the white allele shows white. It never gives blue eyes, whatever the white form does.
Fdk/n · Fdk/Fdc · Fdk/Fdk Fracture Magical Ground and strong white no breed Black Fracture The same marking as the white form with the light taken out of it: a single near-black tone.
Fdf/Fdc · Fdf/Fdk · Fdf/Fdf Fracture Magical Ground and strong white no breed Fracture Instead of white appearing on a coloured horse, the colour appears on a white one - and it does it in bold uneven blocks and streaks with jagged edges, anywhere at all: neck, shoulder, barrel, hindquarter, legs, face. There is no symmetry to it and no pattern to predict; two Fracture horses out of the same parents look nothing like each other. The head usually keeps a distinct area of coat, and the mane and tail follow whatever is underneath them. It takes two copies - a single copy is carried and shows nothing.
Clvc/n · Clvc/Clvc Cleave Magical Ground and strong white no breed Coloured Cleave, front half The horse is split across the barrel and the FRONT half carries the marking, driven into the back half by straight-sided teeth, with a few spears floating clear of the line.
Clvk/n · Clvk/Clvc · Clvk/Clvk Cleave Magical Ground and strong white no breed Black Cleave, front half The horse is split across the barrel and the FRONT half carries the marking, driven into the back half by straight-sided teeth, with a few spears floating clear of the line.
Clvw/n · Clvw/Clvc · Clvw/Clvk · Clvw/Clvw Cleave Magical Ground and strong white no breed Cleave, front half The horse is split across the barrel and the FRONT half carries the marking, driven into the back half by straight-sided teeth, with a few spears floating clear of the line.
Pnc/Pnc Panda Magical Ground and strong white no breed Coloured Panda The same regions, with the ground in a colour the line carries rather than in white. Fully recessive: it takes two copies, and a single copy beside the white allele shows white. It never gives blue eyes, whatever the white form does.
Pnk/n · Pnk/Pnc · Pnk/Pnk Panda Magical Ground and strong white no breed Black Panda The same marking as the white form with the light taken out of it: a single near-black tone.
Pan/n · Pan/Pnc · Pan/Pnk · Pan/Pan Panda Magical Ground and strong white no breed Panda White takes a band right round the middle of the horse - the belly and barrel, wrapping the whole circumference - and carries on over the neck, the head and the tail. The coat survives at the two ends of that band and nowhere else along the body: the shoulder in front of it, the butt and croup behind it, and all four legs the whole way down. The head is white apart from the ears, the muzzle and the patches round the eyes, which keep the coat and are what make it a panda; muzzle and ear markings are optional and the eye patches are a little stylised. Freckles of coat run along the top of the white band - small, or larger and fawn-like. The edges of the coloured regions are smooth: nothing about Panda is jagged or roaned, and no other marking shows on the white.
Gxy/n · Gxy/Gxy Galaxy Magical Ground and strong white no breed Galaxy Black lies over seven to nine tenths of the horse - head, neck, barrel, hindquarter and the upper legs - and the coat survives only as a handful of irregular lobed patches, hard-edged, mostly on the lower legs, the belly, and parts of the shoulder and hip. Two kinds of star sit in the black. Point stars are small hard circles, scattered unevenly, densest over the barrel and hindquarter and sparse on the legs. Streak stars are long tapering strokes curved along their length, following the body's contours - front to back on the barrel, up and down on the shoulder and hip - and they cluster near the shoulder, the crest and the hindquarter. Both come in white and in one to three cool accent hues. The mane and tail are black too and carry the same two forms, the streaks running with the fall of the hair.
Tmc/Tmc Marble Tobiano Magical Ground and strong white no breed Coloured Marble Tobiano The same veining in a colour the line carries rather than in white. Fully recessive: it takes two copies, and a single copy beside the white allele shows white. It never gives blue eyes, whatever the white form does.
Tmk/n · Tmk/Tmc · Tmk/Tmk Marble Tobiano Magical Ground and strong white no breed Black Marble Tobiano The same marking as the white form with the light taken out of it: a single near-black tone.
Tmr/Tmc · Tmr/Tmk · Tmr/Tmr Marble Tobiano Magical Ground and strong white no breed Marble Tobiano A tobiano that never learned to make a round patch. At its most minimal it is a set of hair-thin white veins running vertically and diagonally down from the topline, translucent enough that the coat reads through them, with a narrow line down the face. At its most extensive it is blocky zones eight inches and more across, fully opaque, with sharp defined edges. In between it flows in body-contoured curves at a few inches wide. It only does this when homozygous; a single copy leaves a horse barely distinguishable from an ordinary one, which is how the whole thing stayed hidden for so long.
Sbc/Sbc Imperial Magical Ground and strong white no breed Coloured Imperial The same cape in a colour the line carries rather than in white - which the marking's own tradition says is not an Imperial at all, but the genetics do not care. Fully recessive: it takes two copies, and a single copy beside the white allele shows white. It never gives blue eyes, whatever the white form does.
Sbk/n · Sbk/Sbc · Sbk/Sbk Imperial Magical Ground and strong white no breed Black Imperial The same marking as the white form with the light taken out of it: a single near-black tone.
Sbi/n · Sbi/Sbc · Sbi/Sbk · Sbi/Sbi Imperial Magical Ground and strong white no breed Imperial A cape or blanket shape: it originates at the withers and extends up and forward, broad across the neck and shoulder and running as far as the poll when the coverage is maximal. Its edges are defined but irregular, and it is never solid - slivers of the coat beneath must show through between the branches, and a horse whose neck is simply white is not an Imperial. The branches always join up somewhere: a full wrap around the neck on a heavily marked horse, or a narrower connection dipping into the chest and under the elbow on a lightly marked one. It only ever expresses as white, which is what separates it from brindle and every other stripe-adjacent marking.
Wng/n Winged Magical Ground and strong white no breed White-Winged One copy puts the bands and spots straight onto the coat with no ground under them - two to five roughly concentric bands following the arc of the ribcage from the shoulder back toward the flank, tapering to points at both ends, with ovoid spots gathered near the band ends and over the shoulder, and a single diagonal band crossing the shoulder from the withers toward the elbow. White forms the core of each band and a desaturated tint runs alongside it. Every edge is hard.
Wng/Wng Winged Magical Ground and strong white no breed Spot-Winged Two copies lay a dark field over the shoulder and barrel first - a saddle shape with a hard lobed boundary - and put the bands and spots inside it in white, at a much higher spot count. The bands often hook: one long band curving back on itself and ending broad.
Vln/n · Vln/Vln Valentine Appaloosa Magical Ground and strong white no breed Valentine Appaloosa The appaloosa arrangements, drawn with hearts instead of round spots. A white blanket lies over the hindquarter and hearts are cut out of it as holes, so the coat shows through each one; outside the blanket the same hearts are laid on the coat in white instead. Every heart stands upright with its point down. How much blanket there is varies a great deal - a scatter of hearts over the hip on one horse, a white sheet full of holes on the next. It carries no colour of its own: whatever the horse's base is, Valentine draws in white on it.
Lcc/Lcc Laciano Magical Ground and strong white no breed Coloured Laciano The same pattern in a colour the line carries rather than in white. Fully recessive: it takes two copies, and a single copy beside the white allele shows white. It never gives blue eyes, whatever the white form does.
Lck/n · Lck/Lcc · Lck/Lck Laciano Magical Ground and strong white no breed Black Laciano The same marking as the white form with the light taken out of it: a single near-black tone.
Lac/n · Lac/Lcc · Lac/Lck · Lac/Lac Laciano Magical Ground and strong white no breed Laciano A spontaneous combination of tobiano and appaloosa, and mistakeable for either on its own. The white patches vary a great deal in size and number but are always gathered along the topline and thin out going down - solid over the crest, the withers, the back and the croup, breaking up over the ribs, and rarely anywhere near the belly. The legs are not white unless something else on the horse makes them so. Spotting sits over the hindquarter. The tail is banded - the raccoon tail - and every Laciano has one, which is the only reliable way to name the marking. Striped hooves are common and a white sclera is possible.
Sntch/n · Sntch/Sntch Suntouched Magical Ground and strong white no breed Suntouched The mane and tail burn molten gold, bright enough to read by in the dark, and the horse casts light on the ground around it as it moves. A haze of gold dust drifts off its body.
Wve/n · Wve/Wve Cleave Side Magical Ground and strong white no breed Cleave side Nothing on its own. It decides which half a Cleave takes.
Bhl/n · Bhl/Bhl Black Holes Magical Ground and strong white no breed Black Holes Three to twelve solid patches a side, longer than they are tall and lying roughly nose-to-tail, with margins that are hard but scalloped rather than smooth. Placement is unrestricted: unlike almost everything else, these can land on the face, the lower legs, the belly midline or across the base of the mane. About a fifth of them carry one to three small dots just outside the margin. The fill is one thing per horse, either the animal's own main coat colour or flat black regardless of it.
Bt/n · Bt/Bt Burn Magical Ground and strong white no breed Burnt Mane and Tail Mane, forelock and tail go black, and nothing on the body changes. Up to two strands in five may hold out and keep the base colour or white, in bundles rather than singly, and they hold out most often at the back of the crest and the far end of the tail.
Bm/n · Bm/Bm Burn Magical Ground and strong white no breed Burnt Moon Black from the hoof up all four legs, ending somewhere between the mid-cannon and just below the knee, and standing a little higher at the front of the leg than the back. A black cap over the muzzle enclosing both nostrils. And, on about three in five, a soft black gradient along the belly midline and under the jaw. Every edge here is soft. The value is the same black on any horse - it does not take the coat into account at all.
Bm/Bt Burn Magical Ground and strong white no breed Full Burn One copy of each allele, and the horse takes both fields at once - legs, muzzle and ventral shading black, and the mane and tail black as well. It counts as one mutation, not two.
Wtb/n · Wtb/Wtb Waterborn Magical Ground and strong white no breed Waterborn Neon-blue streaks run through the mane and tail, blue dust trails from the hooves on the move, a grown horse walks over water instead of sinking into it, and a tamed mare can be milked for a bucket of water.
Cpc/Cpc Cracked Porcelain Magical Ground and strong white no breed Cracked Porcelain, glazed The same crazed plates in a colour the line carries rather than in white. Fully recessive: two copies, and a single copy beside the white allele glazes white.
Cpk/n · Cpk/Cpc · Cpk/Cpk Cracked Porcelain Magical Ground and strong white no breed Cracked Porcelain, glazed, black The same marking as the white form with the light taken out of it: a single near-black tone.
Cpw/n · Cpw/Cpc · Cpw/Cpk · Cpw/Cpw Cracked Porcelain Magical Ground and strong white no breed Cracked Porcelain The horse is white the way a glazed pot is white, and the only thing left of the coat is the crack pattern: a network of fine dark lines between plates a few units across. The channels are the one hard edge in the whole mod - nothing about this gene is soft. Bigger plates mean fewer, longer cracks.
Drsc/Drsc Dragon Shroud Magical Ground and strong white no breed Dragon Shroud, coloured The same plumage in a colour the line carries, and without the piping - a coloured shroud is one tone, which is the difference a person will notice first. Fully recessive.
Drsk/n · Drsk/Drsc · Drsk/Drsk Dragon Shroud Magical Ground and strong white no breed Dragon Shroud, black The same marking as the white form with the light taken out of it: the same 2 steps, moved to the dark end of the scale.
Drsw/n · Drsw/Drsc · Drsw/Drsk · Drsw/Drsw Dragon Shroud Magical Ground and strong white no breed Dragon Shroud Overlapping white plumes cover almost the whole horse, each one outlined in a thin steel grey drawn as a slightly larger disc with the plume taken out of it. A second, much finer spot field is subtracted out of them, and that is what leaves the windows of ordinary coat scattered through the white. The mane and tail take saw waves instead, which read as feathered streamers rather than as hair.
Kow/Kow Koi Magical Ground and strong white no breed Koi, white White against the colour instead of orange against black - the pale variety, and the one that shows the patch edges most clearly. Recessive; two copies.
Kob/Kob Koi Magical Ground and strong white no breed Koi, dark The same patchwork with the bright half taken down to a deep colour instead of orange - a koi read at dusk. Recessive to the orange form; two copies.
Koi/n · Koi/Kow · Koi/Kob · Koi/Koi Koi Magical Ground and strong white no breed Koi Orange against black, in patches a few units across with edges as hard as the vocabulary allows. The two layers use one patch field and its inverse, so every texel is one colour or the other and there is no third tone anywhere on the horse.
Snc/Snc Snow Cloud Magical Ground and strong white no breed Snow Cloud, coloured points The same drifted snow with the points in a colour the line carries instead of in near-black. Fully recessive.
Snk/n · Snk/Snc · Snk/Snk Snow Cloud Magical Ground and strong white no breed Snow Cloud, black points The same marking as the white form with the light taken out of it: the same 2 steps, moved to the dark end of the scale.
Snw/n · Snw/Snc · Snw/Snk · Snw/Snw Snow Cloud Magical Ground and strong white no breed Snow Cloud Snow lies over the horse in drifts with the coat showing through where none settled, and three clouds of dark points are laid over the top - each a speckle field pinned to one part of the body: high on the croup, round the ankles, and across the face in front of the ears.
Twc/Twc Tribal Ward Magical Ground and strong white no breed Tribal Ward, coloured The same ward inked in a colour the line carries. Fully recessive: it takes two copies, and a single one beside the black allele inks black.
Twk/n · Twk/Twc · Twk/Twk Tribal Ward Magical Ground and strong white no breed Tribal Ward, black The same marking as the white form with the light taken out of it: a single near-black tone.
Twd/n · Twd/Twc · Twd/Twk · Twd/Twd Tribal Ward Magical Ground and strong white no breed Tribal Ward The ward inked straight onto the horse in near-black - swirls at hip and shoulder, chevrons down the back, bands round the neck and rings round the legs.
Bpc/Bpc Beetle Pearl Magical Ground and strong white no breed Beetle Pearl, coloured The same black ground with the pearls in a colour the line carries. Fully recessive.
Bpk/n · Bpk/Bpc · Bpk/Bpk Beetle Pearl Magical Ground and strong white no breed Beetle Pearl, black The same marking as the white form with the light taken out of it: the same 2 steps, moved to the dark end of the scale.
Bpw/n · Bpw/Bpc · Bpw/Bpk · Bpw/Bpw Beetle Pearl Magical Ground and strong white no breed Beetle Pearl The ground goes to black before anything else, so the pearls have something to be pearls against - on a chestnut as much as on a black. The spot field is mirrored, so the two flanks match, which is what separates this from an ordinary appaloosa.
Ccv/Ccv Coccinella Marking Magical Ground and strong white no breed Coccinella, variant The same ladybird in a lighter, more saturated red with paler shoulder patches - the same insect in a different season. Fully recessive.
Ccr/n · Ccr/Ccv · Ccr/Ccr Coccinella Marking Magical Ground and strong white no breed Coccinella Marking The ladybird painted onto the horse's own colour: red paisley over the barrel, the seven spots inside it, and a cream patch at each shoulder. The paisley is a ring drawn as an arc rather than a closed circle, which is what gives it the comma shape.
Ijm/Ijm Iridescent Jewel Beetle Coat Magical Ground and strong white no breed Iridescent Jewel, muted The same shell with the spectrum run over a much narrower arc, so it reads as one colour shot with another rather than as a rainbow. Fully recessive.
Ijw/n · Ijw/Ijm · Ijw/Ijw Iridescent Jewel Beetle Coat Magical Ground and strong white no breed Iridescent Jewel Beetle Coat A RAMP along Z - centre of the horse to its right side - carries the hue a long way round the wheel, so the flank, the barrel and the belly are three different colours on one animal and the change is continuous. A ramp only varies colour, never coverage, which is why the sheen covers everything and fades nowhere.
Mmc/Mmc Moth Mantle Magical Ground and strong white no breed Moth Mantle, coloured The same moth on a mantle in a colour the line carries - the source asked for it over an orange coat, and this is how a horse gets there on its own. Fully recessive.
Mmk/n · Mmk/Mmc · Mmk/Mmk Moth Mantle Magical Ground and strong white no breed Moth Mantle, black The same marking as the white form with the light taken out of it: the same 2 steps, moved to the dark end of the scale.
Mmw/n · Mmw/Mmc · Mmw/Mmk · Mmw/Mmw Moth Mantle Magical Ground and strong white no breed Moth Mantle The mantle goes on as a solid ground and the net is drawn into it, so the pattern is the same whatever the horse underneath is doing. Everything else on the animal - tail bands, tail tip, cheeks - is drawn in the same ink as the net, which is what ties a set of unrelated marks into one moth.
Owm/Owm Opal Wing-Vein Marking Magical Ground and strong white no breed Opal Wing-Vein, muted The same wing with the glow between the veins turned right down, so it reads as a veined coat rather than as a lit membrane. Fully recessive.
Owv/n · Owv/Owm · Owv/Owv Opal Wing-Vein Marking Magical Ground and strong white no breed Opal Wing-Vein Marking The membrane is a RAMP up the horse rather than a mask, so it fades nowhere and covers everything. The veins are a repeated saw band with a small amplitude, which makes them fan rather than run parallel; the glow is the same mask inverted, so the lit membrane is exactly what the veins are not.
Csh/n · Csh/Csh Carapace Sheen Magical Ground and strong white no breed Carapace Sheen A glassy shell-sheen over the whole coat, deepest on the planes that face the light and palest underneath, with a bright line along the spine and three pale blazes on each flank.
Ssk/n · Ssk/Ssk Smoke Scroll Magical Ground and strong white no breed Ink smoke Black smoke scrolls over the back and down the legs, fringed in grey and trailing white wisps at their edges.
Ssw/n · Ssw/Ssk · Ssw/Ssw Smoke Scroll Magical Ground and strong white no breed White smoke Pale smoke scrolls over the back and down the legs: white cores, fringed in silver and edged with charcoal wisps.
Ekc/Ekc Endless Knot Magical Ground and strong white no breed Coloured Endless Knot The same knot in a colour the line carries. Fully recessive.
Ekk/n · Ekk/Ekc · Ekk/Ekk Endless Knot Magical Ground and strong white no breed Black Endless Knot The same knot in near-black, which reads on pale, cream and grey coats where the white one vanishes.
Ekn/n · Ekn/Ekc · Ekn/Ekk · Ekn/Ekn Endless Knot Magical Ground and strong white no breed Endless Knot A large white interlaced knot across the croup, its outer arms running a short way down each side.
Plc/Plc Paisley Spill Magical Ground and strong white no breed Coloured Paisley Spill The same splash of teardrops in a colour the line carries. Fully recessive.
Plk/n · Plk/Plc · Plk/Plk Paisley Spill Magical Ground and strong white no breed Black Paisley Spill The same splash of teardrops in near-black, which shows on the pale coats the white form vanishes into.
Pls/n · Pls/Plc · Pls/Plk · Pls/Pls Paisley Spill Magical Ground and strong white no breed Paisley Spill A white splash of paisley teardrops breaking over the top of the rump, spilling forward along the barrel and running down the hind legs.
Cs/n · Cs/Cs Cosmic Magical Fields and regions no breed Cosmic The blanket starts at the dorsal midline and spreads down and out from it, one continuous region with no holes in it and no islands off it. It covers the back and the barrel, hangs off the crest for the first half of the neck, and reaches a little way onto the top of each leg - but it does not stop anywhere: it thins out and is simply gone before the poll and before the knee, so there is no edge on it at either end. Its lower boundary on the barrel is the same long fade. Inside are stars, thickest near the topline and thinning with the field itself, with a planet or a spiral galaxy on a heavily marked horse. Where the blanket meets the mane or the tail base the colour runs on into the hair, deepening toward the tips.
Gth/n Goth Magical Fields and regions no breed Goth A hood a shade or several below the coat, running along the top of the neck from the poll back toward the withers, and taking the ears with it. It follows the crest rather than wrapping the neck - down the upper side and no further, never onto the throat - and it stays off the face entirely. The edge is soft on every horse; whether it runs as a smooth curve or a tattered one varies. There is nothing darker than black, so a black horse carrying one copy shows nothing at all.
Gth/Gth Goth Magical Fields and regions no breed Goth, greyed Two copies take the hood the other way: instead of going darker it depigments, and the same crest and ears end up light grey to white. It shows on every base, black included, which the darker form cannot. Either way it lies along the top and upper side of the neck, stops well above the throat, and does not reach the face.
Msk/n Masked Magical Fields and regions no breed Masked carrier One copy is a narrow dark band across the forehead and around the eyes and nothing else - no crest field, no streaks, no tail.
Msk/Msk Masked Magical Fields and regions no breed Masked Dark over the whole head - forehead, poll, orbits, cheeks and jaw, carried down to the bottom of the head and onto the muzzle, with both surfaces of both ears to match. The field runs back along the crest, dips lowest at the mid-neck and rises again at the withers, and stops there - the barrel, flank and legs are untouched. Four to ten light streaks run front to back through the neck field, tapering to points at both ends and irregularly spaced, and they are not mirrored side to side. The far half or two-thirds of the tail is dark, cut square across.
Dtc/Dtc Dutch Magical Fields and regions no breed Coloured Dutch The same band, muzzle and stockings, in a colour the line carries rather than in white. Fully recessive - two copies, and a single copy beside the white allele shows white. It never gives blue eyes.
Dtk/n · Dtk/Dtc · Dtk/Dtk Dutch Magical Fields and regions no breed Black Dutch The same marking as the white form with the light taken out of it: a single near-black tone.
Dut/n · Dut/Dtc · Dut/Dtk · Dut/Dut Dutch Magical Fields and regions no breed Dutch The rabbit pattern. A band wraps the body at the shoulder and chest, front edge at the base of the neck and back edge at the girth, broadest underneath and pinching as it climbs to the withers - and on a minimal horse it fails to close over the topline at all, leaving a bridge of coat there. White runs forward along the underside of the neck to the throat, takes the muzzle, and often a stripe up the face. All four legs are stocked to somewhere between mid-cannon and the knee, and the front and hind heights rarely match. Every edge is hard - no roaning, no feathering - and every one of them is a smooth curve. The barrel, flank, hindquarters, mane and tail keep the coat entirely.
Hpa/n Hued Pangare Magical Fields and regions no breed Classic Hued Pangare Pangare's own field, in colour rather than in cream, at about the strength a moderate mealy horse shows: the muzzle and the rings round the eyes, a coloured underline up the belly, soft patches behind the elbows and in front of the stifle, and a little way up the inside of each leg. Every boundary is a soft mottled fade - there are no edges on this marking - and the mane, tail, ears and topline are left alone, because pangare is a soft-parts trait and so is this.
Hpa/Hpa Hued Pangare Magical Fields and regions no breed Extreme Hued Pangare The same field at full strength: the colour reaches up the flanks and the sides of the neck, over the cheek, jaw and muzzle and round the eye, and most of the way up the inside of all four legs. Half the horse or better. It is not a different shape from the classic form - it is the same map turned all the way up, which is what pangare itself does between a trace and a strong one.
Nbp/Nbp Nebula Points Magical Fields and regions no breed Nebula Points, pigmented The same arrangement in ordinary pigment - no glow, and a colour the line carries rather than the bioluminescent blue. Fully recessive.
Nbg/n · Nbg/Nbp · Nbg/Nbg Nebula Points Magical Fields and regions no breed Nebula Points The points go to black first and everything else is drawn over them, so the light has something to be light against. The fan up the neck is a band on a saw wave, which is what makes it lie along the crest rather than wrap round the throat; the tail is a ramp from near-black at the dock to pale at the tip. All of it is emissive, so a nebula horse is visible at night.
WrM/n · WrM/WrM War Mask Magical Fields and regions no breed War Mask A symmetrical field covering three-fifths to three-quarters of the face, from the poll down through the orbits and the nasal region to the muzzle, bounded along the cheekbone and the jaw. One copy looks exactly like two. Which way it goes depends on the coat underneath: on anything but black it comes in near-black and reads as armour plate, and on a black horse it comes in mid grey instead, because there is nothing darker than black to make a mask out of.
Xuk/n · Xuk/Xuk Xuke Magical Fields and regions no breed Xuke Colour laid over the whole face from poll to muzzle and over every leg from the top to the hoof, dense enough to read as a roan rather than as a scatter of dots - the base coat is obscured, not spotted. The hooves stay black. Some horses also carry striping over the hindquarter, following the muscle, and it is likelier on a horse whose coat is one flat colour. Mane and tail are untouched.
Qzc/Qzc Quazars Magical Fields and regions no breed Coloured quazars The same beams in a colour the line carries. Fully recessive: two copies, and a single copy beside the white allele shows white. It never gives blue eyes.
Qzk/n · Qzk/Qzc · Qzk/Qzk Quazars Magical Fields and regions no breed Black quazars The same marking as the white form with the light taken out of it: a single near-black tone.
Qzr/n · Qzr/Qzc · Qzr/Qzk · Qzr/Qzr Quazars Magical Fields and regions no breed Quazars Spindle-shaped beams, widest a little past halfway along and tapering both ways, laid roughly along the body axis and carrying on down the legs wherever one crosses a shoulder or a stifle. They have no boundary anywhere - the value peaks at the centre line of the beam and falls away steadily to nothing - which is what separates a quazar from a stripe. A beam that reaches the topline carries on down the other side at a little under its original width.
Tdc/Tdc Tidemark Magical Fields and regions no breed Tidemark, coloured The same waterline with the fill and the accent in two colours the line carries. Fully recessive.
Tdk/n · Tdk/Tdc · Tdk/Tdk Tidemark Magical Fields and regions no breed Tidemark, black The same marking as the white form with the light taken out of it: a single near-black tone, and the coloured accents unchanged.
Tdm/n · Tdm/Tdc · Tdm/Tdk · Tdm/Tdm Tidemark Magical Fields and regions no breed Tidemark One sine wave, three times over: the fill below it, the line at it, and a band just above it with a spot field multiplied in to break the band into dots. Because all three read the same wave, the dots follow the line exactly however the arch is drawn - which is the point, and is not something three separate masks could promise.
Shc/Shc Shark Magical Fields and regions no breed Coloured shark The same fin in a colour the line carries. Fully recessive: two copies, and a single copy beside the white allele shows white. It never gives blue eyes.
Shbk/n · Shbk/Shc · Shbk/Shbk Shark Magical Fields and regions no breed Black shark The same marking as the white form with the light taken out of it: a single near-black tone.
Shk/n · Shk/Shc · Shk/Shbk · Shk/Shk Shark Magical Fields and regions no breed Shark One field, and only one: a sickle standing on the topline over the withers, its apex set forward of the middle of its base, its back edge scooped hollow and its front edge straight or slightly bellied. It stands on the base of the neck, which goes pale over the crest and down the upper side but never onto the throat - that much of the neck is part of the marking, not a separate one. Crisp along the top and the back edge, softer underneath. Most sharks also carry a narrow band running forward along the crest from the apex to the poll.
Bsk/Bsk Banded Socks Magical Fields and regions no breed Banded Socks A repeating band across each leg, drawn in part space so a hind leg and a fore leg get the same number of rings rather than the same ring height. Below the knee it reads as a set of stacked socks; the number of them is one knob.
Sky/n · Sky/Sky Sky Shadow Magical Fields and regions no breed Sky Shadow Not a pattern at all - there is no boundary anywhere on it. The coat's value is pulled a quarter to nearly half of the way to mid-grey, which flattens the contrast between the horse and anything else marking it. On top of that the ventral barrel, the flank and the back of the shoulder lighten further, falling off over a hand's span or two. The mane, the tail, the legs from the knee and hock down and the muzzle are left alone at full value, and the transition into them is as gradual as everything else here.
Nim/n · Nim/Nim Nimbus Magical Fields and regions no breed Nimbus A cloud, and mostly a very faint one. The great majority of the marking is a haze you can read the coat straight through, spread over the trunk and trailing onto the head and the legs as a few straggling tangents rather than as a field. Somewhere on the barrel or the shoulder is the body of the cloud - a small dense knot, a hand's span or two across, where the colour actually arrives - and inside that the saturation curdles into stronger and weaker cells so the coat still reads through it in places. There is no boundary to find anywhere on any of it. The whole thing is turbulent: nothing about it repeats, and nothing about it lines up with the horse.
Iua/n · Iua/Iua Iuari Magical Fields and regions no breed Iuari Patches that read as deliberate focal points rather than as marks: darker than the coat on some horses and lighter on others, concentrated on the neck, shoulder, barrel and hindquarter and carrying on down the legs, with soft indistinct middles. What names the marking is the rim - each patch is outlined by speckling that crowds at its boundary and thins away from it, so the patch looks lit from the edge. It goes under white markings rather than over them, and the whole horse still carries its ordinary speckling as well.
Emi/n · Emi/Emi Emitola Magical Fields and regions no breed Emitola Black across the chest, belly and between the legs, extensive and intricate, reaching up the flanks and taking all four legs whole - hoof to stifle, with no break anywhere along the underline and none up a limb either. Its upper boundary is not a line but a row of flames - sharp, jagged, energetic - and the flame work is picked out throughout in one bright speckling colour, which also takes the forelock and the hooves. It goes over white markings rather than under, and the mane is left solid with no striping.
Dsc/Dsc Dorsal Shield Magical Fields and regions no breed Dorsal Shield, coloured The same plate and studs in a colour the line carries. Fully recessive.
Dsk/n · Dsk/Dsc · Dsk/Dsk Dorsal Shield Magical Fields and regions no breed Dorsal Shield, black The same marking as the white form with the light taken out of it: the same 2 steps, moved to the dark end of the scale.
Dsh/n · Dsh/Dsc · Dsh/Dsk · Dsh/Dsh Dorsal Shield Magical Fields and regions no breed Dorsal Shield Two crossed AXIS bands in body units make the plate, so it sits at a fixed place on the horse rather than at a fixed fraction of it - which is what keeps the front and back edges off the shoulder and the croup. Every stud layer carries the same two bands, so nothing escapes the plate.
Osc/n · Osc/Osc Oscar Magical Fields and regions no breed Oscar A very heavy sooty that turns the coat black from head to toe, and it does it on anything - single cream, double cream, pearl, champagne, none of them stop it. Then the black flakes: large wavy stripes lift away and reveal the horse's real genetic colour underneath, whatever that is, and they run mostly upright rather than along the body. At its quietest that is a black horse with a little colour showing on the neck; at its loudest it is half and half, with flecks of dark in the colour and flecks of colour in the dark. Mane and tail follow whatever the flaking does where they meet it. It sits under every white marking. One copy looks exactly like two.
Opc/Opc Opossum Magical Fields and regions no breed Coloured Opossum The same marking in a colour the line carries rather than in white. Fully recessive: it takes two copies, and a single copy beside a white allele shows white. It never gives blue eyes, whatever the white form does.
Opo/n · Opo/Opc · Opo/Opo Opossum Magical Fields and regions no breed Opossum A restricted grey, and restricted to the front of the horse. White hair comes in over the brow and forehead and can carry back across the face, down the neck, over the chest and the point of the shoulder and down the front legs - how far is settled once and does not move afterwards. It stops at the shoulder: the barrel, the flank, the hindquarter and the hind legs are never involved. Where it reaches the muzzle the skin there goes soft pink whether or not a white marking is present. Every edge is roaned and jagged - heavy sabino, not a cut line - and it affects the mane wherever it touches. The skin around the eyes stays dark. It sits under all white markings, and a bald face or a big blaze simply overrides it. One copy looks exactly like two.
Sp/Sp · Gr/Sp Papillon Magical Fields and regions no breed Spotty Papillon The wing breaks up into spotting toward its lower edge rather than ending as a field - the pattern is still the wing's, but read through a scatter of it.
Gr/Gr · Si/Gr Papillon Magical Fields and regions no breed Gradient Papillon The wing fades into the neck's own colour as it descends, brightest at the crest and gone by the throat - blended rather than bounded, and the subtler of the two shaped forms.
Si/Sp · Si/Si Papillon Magical Fields and regions no breed Simple Papillon The wing pattern and the hair colouring, and nothing else - no gradient, no spotting. A clean graphic wing on the neck, and the cleanest of the three to read.
Stt/Stt Stardust Magical Fields and regions no breed Coloured Stardust The same marking in a colour the line carries rather than in white. Fully recessive: it takes two copies, and a single copy beside a white allele shows white. It never gives blue eyes, whatever the white form does.
Str/n · Str/Stt · Str/Str Stardust Magical Fields and regions no breed Stardust Depigmentation only - nothing anywhere gets darker. It starts as a pale yellow-tinted patch at the base of the forelock, at the very end of the tail, or both. From the tail it works up toward the dock and then forward along the spine, spreading sideways over the croup and rump as it goes; from the forelock it works back across the poll and down the neck on a slope, and stops at the shoulder. It never reaches the barrel, the flank or the legs, and the mane is never fully taken - some base-coloured hair always survives in it. Around the edge of everything it has claimed sit the dapples: little radial stars of four to eight tapering points, crowded at the boundary and thinning inward, with fine ticking in the same band. Newly-pale hair carries a warm yellow cast; older ground is pure white.
Acc/Acc Accretion Magical Fields and regions no breed Coloured Bottom Accretion The underside field in a colour the line carries rather than pale. Fully recessive at this locus: it takes two copies, and a single copy beside the pale allele shows pale. It never gives blue eyes, whatever the pale form does.
Acr/n · Acr/Acc · Acr/Acr Accretion Magical Fields and regions no breed Bottom Accretion The pale field is the underside: a quarter to nearly half the horse, bounded by a single line running nose-to-tail at a third to a half of the local body depth measured up from the belly, rising a little over the shoulder and rather more over the flank. The boundary undulates gently and has no edge at all - it is a gradient a hand's span or two wide. All four legs are included the whole way to the hoof with no separate boundary of their own, and about three horses in five also take a cap on the muzzle.
Qc/Qc Quarter Magical Fields and regions no breed Coloured Quarter The same marking in a colour the line carries rather than in white. Fully recessive: it takes two copies, and a single copy beside a white allele shows white. It never gives blue eyes, whatever the white form does. It mirrors Double Quarter rather than Part Quarter: a horse showing the coloured form carries no white allele at all, so there is no second shape left to tell apart.
Qd/n · Qd/Qc · Qd/Qd Quarter Magical Fields and regions no breed Double Quarter Half the trunk rather than a quarter of it: either both front quadrants, both back, both upper or both lower, so one of the two cutting planes disappears and the two quadrants read as a single field with no seam where they meet. Between a third and three-fifths of the horse. Which plane survives and which side of it is pale are both settled at birth.
Qp/n · Qp/Qc · Qp/Qd · Qp/Qp Quarter Magical Fields and regions no breed Part Quarter One contiguous quadrant of the trunk, a fifth to a third of the horse. Two planes cut it: one square across the body axis, one level through it, and the quadrant the pale field takes is settled at birth and does not move. The transverse boundary sits square to the body and the level one runs the length of it, and the two do not meet in a corner - the intersection rounds off over a wide radius because both boundaries are long soft gradients rather than lines. The quadrant may be filled anywhere from three-fifths to fully, and where it falls short the gap is taken from the corner furthest from its centre.
Ffm/n Flametouched Magical Fields and regions no breed Flametouched, flames The flame form: stylised tongues of fire climbing all four legs and a marking across the face, in two or three colours, with the tips brightest. It never touches the topline and never shows over white. It also sets nothing on fire.
Ffm/Ffm Flametouched Magical Fields and regions no breed Flametouched, gradient The gradient form: shading over the topline, the face, the hooves and the mane and tail, running smoothly through two or three colours from ember to flame. It is the only form allowed to touch the topline. Up to half the horse. It sets nothing on fire.
Pat/n · Pat/Pat Patina Magical Fields and regions no breed Patina Oxidation, in the pattern copper takes - and in exactly the places a mealy horse goes pale, because those are the parts of a coat the sun and the sweat get at: the muzzle and jaw, the rings round the eyes, the belly and flank, behind the elbow, up the inside of the legs, and into the mane and tail. Where pangare's boundary is a soft fade this one is speckled all the way along it, so the green arrives as grains rather than as a wash. Dominant, so wherever it is present it shows, but how much varies enormously from horse to horse: a few faint traces on one, heavy verdigris marbling on the next. It also mottles the skin round the eyes and muzzle. A dark coat hides most of it, which is why it is often said not to exist on black horses; a diluted one shows it better, and a double cream or pearl coat hides it completely while passing it on intact.
Abt/Abt · Atp/Abt · Atp/Atp Accretion Field Magical Fields and regions no breed Wild type Nothing, on its own - and that is the whole of what this locus does by itself. It exists to be read by Accretion, which asks it which way round to draw: one copy of Atp puts the pale field on the topline, and a horse with none of it takes the underside. A horse carrying no Accretion at all still carries this, still breeds it, and still passes it on - which is why a pair of underside-marked parents can throw a topline foal without either of them ever having shown one.
Lbc/Lbc Ladybug Saddle Magical Fields and regions no breed Ladybug Saddle, coloured The same saddle with the panels in a colour the line carries - the pink and red end of the wheel, which is where a ladybird lives. Fully recessive.
Lbs/n · Lbs/Lbc · Lbs/Lbs Ladybug Saddle Magical Fields and regions no breed Ladybug Saddle The panel is one shape and the spine is drawn back through it, rather than two panels drawn separately - which is what guarantees the split is straight and central however the panel is sized. The veins are a crackle field clipped to the panel, so they stop where it does.
Agc/Agc Agate Magical Fields and regions no breed Agate, chroma The same cracked mosaic, but every cell takes its own colour - pastel at the rim, deepening toward the middle, veined through with dark cracks.
Agt/n · Agt/Agc · Agt/Agt Agate Magical Fields and regions no breed Agate, opal A pearly cracked-opal sheen - pale cells rimmed faintly in lilac-white, brightening to a milky glow at each cell's heart.
Moc/Moc Morpho Wing Magical Fields and regions no breed Morpho Wing, coloured The same folded wing, but the light in it is the colour this line of horses runs to - still barred in black.
Mok/n · Mok/Moc · Mok/Mok Morpho Wing Magical Fields and regions no breed Morpho Wing, black The same marking as the white form with the light taken out of it: the same 2 steps, moved to the dark end of the scale.
Mow/n · Mow/Moc · Mow/Mok · Mow/Mow Morpho Wing Magical Fields and regions no breed Morpho Wing, pale A soft silver-white sheen pools low on the haunch, banded with a few dark bars raking back toward the tail.
Crb/n · Crb/Crb Coral Bloom Magical Fields and regions no breed Coral Bloom The coat breaks into overlapping ruffled lobes, deep sea-blue at the rim and shading to a pale crown at the middle, divided by seams of the horse's own colour and flecked with tiny gold specks.
Gbl/Gbl Gill Bloom Magical Fields and regions no breed Gill Bloom One to a few fan-shaped patches across the barrel, each with a matte gold rim opening onto a deep maroon centre spoked with dark gills.
Ibd/n · Ibd/Ibd Iris Bloom Diamonds Magical Fields and regions no breed Iris Bloom Diamonds Dark royal-blue diamonds spread across the barrel, denser and smaller with a second copy. Each is rimmed in near-black, dappled white through its outer half, and lit at the middle by radiating amber veins.
Kbc/Kbc Kite Bloom Magical Fields and regions no breed Kite Bloom, coloured Bold facets tile the coat edge to edge, every panel its own deep colour and every one dusted with small pale flecks.
Kbk/n · Kbk/Kbc · Kbk/Kbk Kite Bloom Magical Fields and regions no breed Kite Bloom, black The same marking as the white form with the light taken out of it: a single near-black tone.
Kbw/n · Kbw/Kbk · Kbw/Kbw Kite Bloom Magical Fields and regions no breed Kite Bloom, pale A faint silvery lattice of panels ghosts over the coat, catching the light without much colour in it.
Kbw/Kbc Kite Bloom Magical Fields and regions no breed Kite Bloom, washed The lattice shows through more clearly, each panel tinted with a muted wash of colour under the pale overlay.
Mnc/Mnc Moonwing Magical Fields and regions no breed Moonwing, coloured The same wings, but the field itself carries deep colour instead of white - the same dust, the same gold binding.
Mnk/n · Mnk/Mnc · Mnk/Mnk Moonwing Magical Fields and regions no breed Moonwing, black The same marking as the white form with the light taken out of it: a single near-black tone, and the coloured accents unchanged.
Mnw/n · Mnw/Mnc · Mnw/Mnk · Mnw/Mnw Moonwing Magical Fields and regions no breed Moonwing, pearl Twin pearl-white wings sweep up over the topline and down both flanks, glimmering with a faint blue-violet-pink dust and bound at the edge by a thick band of gold - and every other white marking the horse carries is gilded and dusted to match.
Nbs/n · Nbs/Nbs Nightbloom Shroud Magical Fields and regions no breed Nightbloom Shroud The crest, poll and skull glow deep abyssal blue, and ribbons of gold-to-violet gill striping fan down the neck and barrel, dimming to the ordinary coat above the legs.
Ocp/Ocp Opal Crackle Magical Fields and regions no breed Opal Crackle, pigmented The same cracked network, but the seams glow one steady colour instead of shifting cell to cell.
Ocr/n · Ocr/Ocp · Ocr/Ocr Opal Crackle Magical Fields and regions no breed Opal Crackle, iridescent A network of dark, thin-walled cracks runs the length of the coat, every seam lit from within and none of them agreeing about the colour.
Opd/Opd Opaline Magical Fields and regions no breed Opaline, deep The coat breaks into a stained-glass mosaic of blue and violet cells, each ringed in near-black and lit with tiny pale bubbles where the veins meet.
Opw/n · Opw/Opd · Opw/Opw Opaline Magical Fields and regions no breed Opaline, white A faint webbing of pale glassy veins divides the coat into soft cream-on-cream cells, like frost on a window.
Frs/n · Frs/Frs Orchid Frost Magical Fields and regions no breed Orchid Frost Pale, soft-edged blotches spread over the coat, bleaching the darker parts toward pink and passing over anything already that light. Two copies frost it heavily; one leaves a scatter.
Ptl/n · Ptl/Ptl Petaled Magical Fields and regions no breed Petaled The coat over the body, neck, head and muzzle breaks into overlapping petals - a thin dark rose edge, then a thick black line, then a pale blush field veined in rose toward the middle.
Tfs/n · Tfs/Tfs Tidefoam Sash Magical Fields and regions no breed Tidefoam Sash A diagonal sash of marbled sea-teal crosses the ribcage, its surface broken by a fine white network of foam cells with occasional larger bubbles caught in it.
Twc/Twc Tidewave Magical Fields and regions no breed Coloured tidewave Three long swells running the length of the ribs, the colour sweeping most of the wheel from tail to nose.
Tw/n · Tw/Twc · Tw/Tw Tidewave Magical Fields and regions no breed Pale tidewave Three long white swells running the length of the ribs, stepped from bright to grey.
Trc/Trc Trillium Magical Fields and regions no breed Coloured trillium Three petals in the line's own colour around a ring of a second colour and a fixed amber eye.
Trb/n · Trb/Trc · Trb/Trb Trillium Magical Fields and regions no breed Black trillium Three near-black petals on the barrel, the ring and the eye stepped darker still.
Tri/n · Tri/Trc · Tri/Trb · Tri/Tri Trillium Magical Fields and regions no breed Pale trillium Three off-white petals on the barrel around a grey ring and a darker eye.
Bwc/Bwc Barred Wing Magical Fields and regions no breed Coloured wing Wings in the line's own colour, barred in a second.
Bwb/n · Bwb/Bwc · Bwb/Bwb Barred Wing Magical Fields and regions no breed Black wing Near-black wings across the ribs, barred in a mid grey.
Bw/n · Bw/Bwc · Bw/Bwb · Bw/Bw Barred Wing Magical Fields and regions no breed Pale wing Grey wings across the ribs, barred in white.
Urc/Urc Uraniid Magical Fields and regions no breed Coloured uraniid A moth in the line's own colour, with two bands in a second.
Urb/n · Urb/Urc · Urb/Urb Uraniid Magical Fields and regions no breed Black uraniid A near-black moth on the barrel, raked with mid-grey bands.
Ur/n · Ur/Urc · Ur/Urb · Ur/Ur Uraniid Magical Fields and regions no breed Pale uraniid A grey moth on the barrel, raked with white bands.
Cnc/Cnc Candelabra Magical Fields and regions no breed Coloured candelabra A chandelier in the line's own colour.
Cnb/n · Cnb/Cnc · Cnb/Cnb Candelabra Magical Fields and regions no breed Black candelabra A near-black chandelier standing on the ribs.
Cnd/n · Cnd/Cnc · Cnd/Cnb · Cnd/Cnd Candelabra Magical Fields and regions no breed Pale candelabra A bone-white chandelier standing on the ribs.
Tfc/Tfc Taper Flame Magical Fields and regions no breed Coloured flame A taper flame in the line's own colour, with a spark of a second near the tip.
Tfb/n · Tfb/Tfc · Tfb/Tfb Taper Flame Magical Fields and regions no breed Black flame A near-black taper flame on the ribs, stepped up to a mid-grey foot.
Tf/n · Tf/Tfc · Tf/Tfb · Tf/Tf Taper Flame Magical Fields and regions no breed Pale flame A grey taper flame on the ribs, palest at its foot.
Ikc/Ikc Inkcoil Magical Fields and regions no breed Coloured coil A brushed coil in the line's own colour, washed with a lighter tone of the same.
Ikb/n · Ikb/Ikc · Ikb/Ikb Inkcoil Magical Fields and regions no breed Black coil A near-black brushed coil up the ribs, with a mid-grey wash trailing it.
Ik/n · Ik/Ikc · Ik/Ikb · Ik/Ik Inkcoil Magical Fields and regions no breed Pale coil A grey brushed coil up the ribs, with a lighter wash trailing it.
C/C Foxglove Magical Fields and regions no breed Coloured bells A stalk of bells in the line's own colour, dark-throated and lit along their shoulders.
B/n · B/C · B/B Foxglove Magical Fields and regions no breed Nightbell The same stalk in mourning greys, near-black bells over a black stem.
W/n · W/C · W/B · W/W Foxglove Magical Fields and regions no breed Foxglove The reference form - a slate stem hung with white speckled bells.
Opc/Opc Opal Fire Magical Fields and regions no breed Opal fire (coloured) A dark patch on the shoulder with two sprays of coloured fire fanning up out of it, the spectrum running from the roots to the tips.
Opw/n · Opw/Opc · Opw/Opw Opal Fire Magical Fields and regions no breed Opal fire (white) A dark patch on the shoulder with two sprays of white fire fanning up out of it, graded from bright at the root to grey at the tips.
Aur/n · Aur/Aur Aurorae Magical Spots and rings no breed Aurorae The light is in the hair and nowhere else: the lower half or third of the mane and of the tail take it, brightening the whole way to the ends, and the coat itself is untouched. One copy lights the tips alone; two carry it further up the strands and brighter. How far up varies from horse to horse, and the boundary is a fade rather than a line - there is nothing on this marking you could put a finger on.
Gl/n · Gl/Gl Glow Magical Spots and rings no breed Glow Fine luminescent speckles densest over the neck, shoulder, ribcage and hindquarter and thinning toward the belly, with all four hooves lit in the same colour. One hue per horse, with the speckles a shade paler than the hooves. The mane and tail are untouched.
Ang/Ang Angler Magical Spots and rings no breed Angler One string of lights down each side of the horse, and only one: a single line that runs the length of the barrel and carries on up the side of the neck, rising and falling in a smooth even curve you can follow with a finger. The two sides match each other light for light. Each light is a round or oval pale core inside a deeper rim of the same hue, and one horse carries one design throughout. Mane, tail, face, legs and hooves are untouched however close the string passes.
La/n · La/La Lantern Magical Spots and rings no breed Lantern A line of light along the spine, brightest at its core and dissipating into a soft bloom either side. It breaks in places - a lantern horse rarely carries an unbroken line - and the same colour shows faintly through the mane where the line passes under it. One copy is as lit as two: the horse cannot turn it off, and in the dark it is the only part of it you see.
Rs/n Rosette Magical Spots and rings no breed Semi-rosette One copy scatters the rosettes over the upper half of the horse - clear and well-drawn, but selectively placed, and rarely reaching below the barrel.
Rs/Rs Rosette Magical Spots and rings no breed Blanket rosette Two copies crowd the rosettes together until they touch, and where they touch they read as broken striping rather than as rings. Coverage runs from average to the whole horse, and the mane picks up the tone wherever a rosette meets the topline.
Wmc/Wmc Wormholes Magical Spots and rings no breed Coloured Wormholes The same marking in a colour the line carries rather than in white. Fully recessive: it takes two copies, and a single copy beside the white allele shows white. It never gives blue eyes, whatever the white form does.
